General Quality, Health, Safety and Environmental Requirements

1.1 COMPANY is committed to conducting operations in a manner which minimizes the risks to the health and safety of those involved in, and affected by, such operations. COMPANY is also committed to conducting its operations in a manner which minimizes the impact on natural and ‘built’ environments in the area of operations.

1.2 This section sets out minimum Quality, Health, Safety and Environmental (QHSE) requirements for the WORK that CONTRACTOR and subcontractors will be required to comply with whilst conducting operations for COMPANY.

1.3 COMPANY may perform audits and assessments of CONTRACTOR’s and its subcontractors’ performance under the CONTRACT. COMPANY reserves the right without prior notice to conduct quality, health, safety, security and environmental audits of the Operations.

1.4 CONTRACTOR shall at all times be responsible for performing the WORK in a manner so as to protect against loss of life, injury to people, damage to property and the environment. The detailed manner means and methods of performing the WORK shall be under the control and direction of CONTRACTOR.

1.5 Prior to the start of Operations, certain QHSE processes must be completed. These QHSE processes include developing the final QHSE plan format and frequency of any QHSE audits, permitting and licensing and COMPANY’s approvals. CONTRACTOR shall ensure that sufficient resources are dedicated to this task to ensure completion prior to mobilization.

1.6 COMPANY may at any time stop the WORK or any portion of the WORK without penalty to

COMPANY if the WORK is not conducted in accordance with the Quality, Health, Safety or

Environmental guidelines specified in this CONTRACT or by the laws of the appropriate

jurisdiction. COMPANY reserves the right to require CONTRACTOR to remove any person from

the Operations in the case of non-compliance with QHSE procedures. COMPANY may also

prohibit the use of equipment and plant in cases where that equipment or plant does not meet

the required standards or is deemed unsafe.

1.7 CONTRACTOR shall ensure that activities associated with this CONTRACT are conducted in

compliance with applicable national and local HSE laws and regulations, CONTRACTOR’s

standards and COMPANY’s requirements and IADC documents.

1.8 Limits of Authority

COMPANY’s Representative(s) do not have the authority to relax health, safety or environment standards. If they judge the Operation to be inadequate with regards to any aspect of quality, health, safety, security or the environment, then the operation may be immediately suspended.

1.9 QHSE Plan

CONTRACTOR shall be required to develop a project-specific QHSE plan to manage the WORK and associated risks. This plan shall, as a minimum, address topics included in the list detailed in Item 7. Where existing CONTRACTOR QHSE publications detail the topics listed, it is sufficient to either include them verbatim, or provide reference to where they may be found in CONTRACTOR’s QHSE Manuals. The final project-specific QHSE plan shall satisfy CONTRACTOR standards, COMPANY requirements and applicable HSE laws and regulations.

1.10 Contractor should establish an effective HSE system and implement it strictly.

1.11 Contractor must adhere to the standards and regulations of Health, Safe and Environment constituted by the local government.

Well : AZNS-118 (G04)

Type : IGH

Health, Safety and Environment Page 143 of 175

Development Well

1.12 Contractor must strictly adhere to the procedures / standards and regulations of Health, Safety and Environment promulgated by NIOC.

1.13 Contractor should comply with all applicable local government regulations including guidelines and recommended practices and any other Iranian authorities having jurisdiction.

1.14 All personnel should respect the local culture, traditions and social values of the indigenous population of the Iran and South Azadegan region.

1.15 Contractor shall hire qualified and experienced worker and familiar his personnel with hazards especially with H2S and Mine and UXO (unexploded ordinance) according to related procedure.

1.16 Contractor shall conduct proper training about UXO (unexploded ordinance) and mine awareness to all personnel

Health &amp;amp; Safety Management

2.1 CONTRACTOR shall take all necessary safety measures in relation to the WORK to be provided and shall conduct itself and its work force in such a way as to comply at all times with the provisions of the national and local health and safety regulations pertinent to the contracted Scope of WORK.

CONTRACTOR shall also comply with all and any of COMPANY’S safety standards and requirements as particularly applicable to the Operating Area and/or as may be imposed by COMPANY from time to time.

2.2 CONTRACTOR and its subcontractors shall take all necessary steps to provide a safe and healthy working environment for employees, COMPANY’s personnel and third parties in the performance of the WORK.

2.3 CONTRACTOR shall operate a management system that ensures:

 CONTRACTOR’s and sub contractors’ personnel operate safe systems of work at all times

for the performance of the WORK.

 Hazards associated with the work are identified and appropriate actions are taken to prevent

accidents.

 Risks to safety and health of personnel involved in the WORK are identified and assessed,

and any actions and measurements are implemented to either eliminate or reduce these

risks to a level that is considered as low as reasonably practicable, given the nature of the

WORK.

 Personnel are aware of the identified hazards and risks to their safety and health and

to the safety and health of others.

The detailed safe working procedures established by CONTRACTOR to ensure that the WORK will be conducted with due regard to the QHSE aspects, shall be detailed or referenced in the QHSE plan. Copies of the source documents shall be provided to COMPANY if the procedures are referenced only.

2.4 QHSE Representative

CONTRACTOR shall provide a dedicated, on-crew QHSE Representative. The primary responsibility of the QHSE Representative shall include, but not be limited to, the maintenance and monitoring of the implementation of HSE guidelines and procedures and where required provide HSE awareness training for the crew. Such activities shall be approved and actively supported by CONTRACTOR at corporate management level.

2.5 Training and Competence

CONTRACTOR shall ensure that its employees and those of its subcontractors are trained and competent to perform the WORK in a safe and environmentally responsible manner. Such

Well : AZNS-118 (G04)

Type : IGH

Health, Safety and Environment Page 144 of 175

Development Well

training and demonstration of competence will include but not be limited to vocational, safety, emergency, environmental and other training required by regulatory agencies and IADC/OGP guidelines. CONTRACTOR’s senior staff shall have received training in safety management.

2.6 Accident/Incident Reporting

2.6.1 CONTRACTOR shall report all incidents. Any hazardous incident / near miss involving

COMPANY, CONTRACTOR’s, or any third party personnel, plant or equipment, shall be

reported to COMPANY, whether or not injury to personnel or damage/loss to plant or equipment

resulted.

The following incidents shall be verbally reported immediately to the on-site COMPANY Representative(s): damage to property or equipment belonging to COMPANY, third parties, CONTRACTOR or its subcontractors; injury to personnel of COMPANY, third parties, subcontractors or CONTRACTOR; near misses which may have had serious potential consequences; environmental damage; media attention. An initial written report, containing factual information only, shall be prepared by CONTRACTOR and delivered to the COMPANY Representative within twenty-four (24) hours of the incident.

COMPANY reserves the right to participate in the investigation of any incident arising out of performance of the WORK.

2.6.2 CONTRACTOR and all subcontractors shall have an accident and incident reporting system, details of which shall be included in the QHSE plan.

2.6.3 CONTRACTOR shall maintain and forward to COMPANY, a monthly summary of its safety performance.

2.6.4 CONTRACTOR shall prepare and forward to COMPANY, a weekly report stating all incidents and near misses that occurred during that week.

2.6.5 All CONTRACTOR and subcontractor personnel and COMPANY Representative shall attend safety meetings on a regular basis. COMPANY Representative shall be given a copy of the minutes of the meetings.

2.7 Safety Equipment &amp;amp; Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) requirements

CONTRACTOR shall ensure that appropriate PPE compliant to national standards is supplied to CONTRACTOR’s personnel involved in the WORK. COMPANY intends to use water-based mud and hence CONTRACTOR’s Personnel that may be exposed to this must be provided with appropriate PPE.

CONTRACTOR shall be solely liable for all costs in equipping its own and all other personnel on contract to, or under the control of, CONTRACTOR with the required safety equipment. CONTRACTOR will provide to all CONTRACTOR’s Personnel appropriate PPE throughout the WORK. Any defective PPE will be replaced without delay. Personnel without the correct PPE will not be allowed to continue work, until replacement PPE has been issued. If any person is disallowed to work owing to inadequate PPE then COMPANY will deduct his day-rate from CONTRACTOR’s invoice.

CONTRACTOR shall detail in the QHSE plan the PPE proposed for issue to the crew. This proposal should include the arrangements for replacement (due to wear and tear and damage) of this equipment.

2.8 Health

2.8.1 This well is committed to develop for oil in a responsible manner that promotes employees' and local inhabitants' health.

2.8.2 A trained Paramedic and proper MEDIVAC equipment and ambulance shall be stationed on the drilling rig during the drilling campaign.

2.8.3 An emergency medical evacuation plan shall be coordinated, and shall keep in touch with emergency response centres manned by qualified doctor 24-hours per day in Tehran or Ahwaz city.

2.8.4 Medical emergency report and investigation shall be performed as soon as possible in accordance with Medical Emergency Procedures.

2.8.5 CONTRACTOR shall, as a minimum, conduct the WORK and provide personnel, equipment, facilities and services consistent with good oil field practices and OGP Guidelines. An assessment of the Contract Area with regard to “Environmental and Occupational Health Risks” and “Local Medical Support” shall be provided by CONTRACTOR in the QHSE plan. This assessment and the resulting “Health Risk Assessment Score” will be discussed with COMPANY, and a final assessment will be agreed.

2.8.6 To ensure the fitness of those personnel engaged to undertake the WORK, CONTRACTOR shall conduct periodic medical examinations of its staff and ensure that its subcontractors’ personnel are also subject to periodic medical examination. As a minimum this shall include pre-hire medical examinations of temporary local personnel and bi-annual medical examinations of its permanent and expatriate staff. Evidence should be made available to COMPANY to verify an appropriate program of immunizations consistent with the recommendations of the World Health Organization for the area of operations.

2.8.7 CONTRACTOR shall be responsible for the cost of medical examinations as required to provide COMPANY with medical certificates which confirm the fitness of CONTRACTOR’s assigned personnel for the WORK. Each drilling rig shall be equipped with a hospital, doctor and an ambulance type-2 level.

2.8 Drug Abuse and Control

2.9.1 CONTRACTOR warrants that it shall obtain valid written consent of all Personnel to a search of

property of such Personnel when COMPANY suspects that such Personnel have in their

possession drugs and/or other prohibited substances or any other item likely to prejudice safety

or security. COMPANY will provide a representative to attend the search.

2.9.2 CONTRACTOR warrants that it shall obtain valid written consent of all Personnel to medical

testing for the presence of drugs or other prohibited substances.

2.9.3 CONTRACTOR further agrees that any Personnel who refuse to provide such consent or

consents or withdraws such consent or consents shall at COMPANY’s option be removed from

the WORK and shall not, at COMPANY’s option, be assigned to any future services to be

provided by CONTRACTOR for COMPANY.

2.9.4 CONTRACTOR further agrees that it shall immediately notify COMPANY in the event that

CONTRACTOR becomes aware that any of its Personnel has been found in possession of

drugs and/or any other prohibited substance. CONTRACTOR is required to notify COMPANY

in the event that any Personnel provided for the WORK have previously been found in

possession, or have abused drugs and/or other substances prohibited at Location. The

acceptance of such Personnel for the WORK shall be at the sole discretion of COMPANY.

2.9 Camp Standards

CONTRACTOR shall abide by all statutory Iranian governmental health and safety regulations, and maintain acceptable standards for cleanliness and hygiene for quarters, camps and the Operating Area(s) in general.

CONTRACTOR shall detail the standards and facilities proposed for its camp in the QHSE plan.

2.10 Emergency Preparedness

CONTRACTOR and its subcontractors shall have emergency preparedness and response plans plus procedures available at all times throughout the duration of the CONTRACT.

CONTRACTOR shall take all necessary measures to ensure that:

I. Potential emergency situations have been identified and assessed;

II. An emergency response plan, including clear responsibilities, is established;

III. CONTRACTOR’s management, employees and third parties are aware of response

procedures and responsibilities;

IV. Regular drills and exercises are carried out to test the emergency response

organization and procedures and that any resultant issues are properly addressed.

CONTRACTOR shall notify COMPANY immediately with respect to any emergency situation and shall provide regular updates on emergency planning procedures, tests etc.

CONTRACTOR shall detail in the QHSE plan a list of potential contingencies for which emergency response procedures are planned. The QHSE plan should also detail a schedule of proposed exercises and drills to verify the effectiveness of the proposed arrangements in place. Development and implementation of the above plan shall incorporate any COMPANY assigned personnel to the project.

Environmental Management

3.1 CONTRACTOR shall comply with any and all laws, rules and regulations of governmental agencies having jurisdiction, which now exist or may be promulgated during the term of the CONTRACT, relating to the control and prevention of damage to the environment.

3.2 Without prejudice to Item 3.1 above, CONTRACTOR shall ensure that all personnel use the highest prevailing standards of care and diligence to prevent unlawful disposal of all waste oil, water and other waste materials or fluids which may accumulate or be caused by CONTRACTOR’s performance of the CONTRACT. CONTRACTOR shall not dispose of any objects, scrap, waste, rubbish, equipment, fluids or fuel into the surrounding area or allow anything to be stored loose where it can be driven into surrounding areas by wind. CONTRACTOR shall take all reasonable precautions to prevent pollution of any nature or kind from its performance of the WORK. CONTRACTOR shall have contingency plans and procedures in place for such events.

3.3 In the event of any disposal prohibited by Item 3.2 above, whether by dumping, spilling, emission or otherwise arising from CONTRACTOR’s performance or non-performance of the CONTRACT, CONTRACTOR shall be fully liable for and bear the costs of removal of any such item(s) dumped, spilled, emitted or otherwise disposed regardless of the sole, concurrent or contributory negligence or breach of duty (statutory or otherwise) of COMPANY. In the event of any such disposal, CONTRACTOR shall immediately undertake all reasonable remedial and protective measures required in such circumstances.

3.4 CONTRACTOR shall notify COMPANY immediately with respect to any pollution, loss, damage,

claim or demand (or occurrence which may give rise to same) resulting from the CONTRACT.

CONTRACTOR shall report to COMPANY any incidents of non-compliance with legislative and

regulatory environmental requirements that occur during the performance of the WORK.

3.5 Without relieving CONTRACTOR of any of its obligations under Item 3.2, if CONTRACTOR does not promptly proceed to perform its obligations under this Item 3, COMPANY may take part to any degree it deems necessary in the control and removal of any pollution, dumping, spillage or contamination which is the responsibility of CONTRACTOR under this Item 3.2, or

which is due to any act or omission of CONTRACTOR. CONTRACTOR shall reimburse COMPANY for costs arising from such actions of COMPANY upon receipt of invoice from COMPANY. COMPANY shall have the right to deduct such costs from any sum due or becoming due to CONTRACTOR under the CONTRACT.

3.6 CONTRACTOR shall operate a management system that is able to demonstrate compliance with environmental laws, rules and regulations applicable to its activities, products and services.

3.7 CONTRACTOR and its subcontractors shall take all necessary steps to ensure that the environmental impacts of their work are responsibly managed and shall take all necessary measures to ensure that:

(i) CONTRACTOR’s personnel are aware of the environmental impact of the CONTRACTOR’s operations and are sufficiently trained and competent to perform the WORK.

(ii) CONTRACTOR’s personnel operate systems, which will ensure that significant environmental impacts of WORK are identified and managed in accordance with a commitment to legal compliance and continual improvement.

(iii) The environmental risks of operations associated with the WORK are evaluated and appropriate actions are taken to prevent accidents and reduce pollution, and that contingency plans are in place in the event of an incident.

(iv) CONTRACTOR will have adequate processes for monitoring the environmental performance of their WORK.

3.8 CONTRACTOR will, as a minimum, conduct the WORK and provide personnel, equipment,

facilities and services consistent with IADC Environmental Guidelines.

CONTRACTOR’s proposals for environmental protection should be detailed in the QHSE Plan. This should include, but not be limited to:

 procedures and precautions to minimize damage to crops and vegetation, the local

ecology and disturbance of wildlife and farm animals;

 recycling and waste disposal;

 Clean-up and restoration of seismic lines, Rig sites access roads and trails, fixed sites

occupied during the survey.

3.9 An ecological team shall be set up by CONTRACTOR, with the purpose of collecting all waste,

rubbish, food scraps, office waste and other discarded material from camps, created by the

WORK. Such activities shall be performed on a daily basis. Such material shall be appropriately

disposed of, according to COMPANY approved methods or existing methods established by

CONTRACTOR, which are proven to be acceptable to COMPANY. Well location and camp site

shall be delivered to COMPANY without any waste, rubbish, food scraps, office waste and other

discarded material. COMPANY has the right to hold Completion certificate till cleaning the well

location and camp site by CONTRACTOR.

3.10 Requirement on location

1) The access road and well location construction shall be accorded with construction

program of well location. It minimizes any impacts on the local residents, prevent all soil

contamination, prevent all ground water contamination, and prevent all surface water

contamination. Cutting and waste water pit shall be lining and drainage system shall be

good and to avoid farm and season canal contamination.

2) Management regulation for transportation truck, drilling operation machine and tools shall

be carried out strictly to avoid damage farm and trees, etc.

3) Well site shall be flat and clean. All the equipment, materials, drilling tools shall be put in

order.

4) Control the rig noise to decrease the influence to surroundings while drilling at the

inhabitation area.

5) Contractor shall perform on site segregation of wastes prior to transfer to the transfer area

and/or delivery to the transportation Contractor in accordance with the following waste

hazard classification.

3.11 Non-Hazardous Wastes (Re-usable, Recyclable and Recoverable Wastes)

1) Ferrous and non-ferrous metals, plastics, and wood wastes, PETs and potentially paper

and glass

2) Materials shall be separately segregated and provision may be made for size reduction and

containerization equipment.

3) The Contractor/Generators are encouraged to seek for recycle/re-use alternatives based

on the Local available facilities, for final disposal of these materials.

4) It should be noted that landfill disposal of metal waste materials and wood wastes shall be

prohibited. For wood wastes re-use of and/or salvage of commercial timber, or

deliver/release to local community are recommended.

3.12 Hazardous Wastes

Hazardous Waste, such as clinic waste, oil waste, waste produced by painting, insulation works shall be duly segregated.

3.13 Facilities of environment protection supplied to well site

1) Mud purifying circulation system

2) Cooling water spraying and circulating system for drilling pump

3) Special recovery tank for deposed oil

4) Reserve house for valuable mud chemicals

5) Automatic mud weighting supply system

6) BOP stack is equipped according to drilling design

7) Define a separation area for gathering all waste according to waste management

procedure

3.14 Requirement of environment protection on location

Sewage pit Area of the sewage pit: 1500 m2

1) Prior to spud， mud pit and sewage pit must comply with the standard. Install cooling water

spraying and circulating system for drilling pump and sewage circulating system for

cleaning rig floor and equipment.

2) Install BOP stack and pressure test shall be operated according to the drilling design to

avoid blowout.

3) Install oil supply lines and check whether there is any leakage.

Well : AZNS-118 (G04)

3.15 Management of environment protection on location

1) In drilling operation ， keep mud and sewage from overflowing. If it happens, clean up

timely.

2) Mud chemicals shall be stored according to the standard and shall not be scattered around

well site. If mud chemicals scatter while loading or unloading, clean it up at once. The sack

of mud chemicals shall be recovered. Do not dispose it on site.

3) Enhance water management at well location and do not wash equipment with fresh water.

Stop water flowing for long time. Overhead tanks shall be utilized.

4) Oil management shall be carefully at the well site. No oil dirt on site, no dripping and

leaking at oil tank and manifold system area.

5) After the well is completed, reinforce the sewage pit ， solidify the disposed mud and have

environment procedure fulfilled.

6) Install fuel supply lines and keep them in good condition.

7) All the drilling fluid materials shall be recovered and shall not be left at the well site after

completion.

8) The environmental protection personnel from contractor and operator side shall check and

accept the well site environment according to the regulation after completion. Only well site

environment is qualified, the well can be delivered.

9) Transition Area for Hazardous Waste shall also be provided by each Contractor in his

allocated area,

10) Company shall approve each area before use as well. The transition area of hazardous

waste shall

11) be:

 Paved, fenced, marked and illuminated

 Equipped with appropriate Fire Extinguisher and other emergency response

equipment (e.g. Eye-Wash and Emergency Shower, appropriate First Aid kits,

neutralizer substances etc.).

 In compliance with the requirements of handling, transportation and storage of

hazardous waste (based on usage chemical MSDS).

 Configured to accommodate a safe physical separation of incompatible waste

materials (e.g. acids, and alkalis, organic materials and strong oxidizing agents).

 Capable enough to provide storage for the entire range of hazardous wastes

generated during the work.

 The area shall be provided with Spill Containment and means of preventing run off

to and from the area.

 All waste containers to be stored shall be appropriately labeled.

 Hazardous Waste shall be transferred in a timely manner, within a maximum period

of 48 hours, for any period in excess of this permission shall be granted from

Company and/or PEDEC.

3.16 Standard of utilization and discharging of wastes on location

1) Conduct drilling operation according to the design, drilling mud is needed before spud.

2) Sewage that cannot be recovered shall be put into the pit do not discharge. If the volume of

sewage is too big and some should be discharged the quality of drainage must comply with

the following regulations.

Maximum permitted concentration of wastes in water

Number Items Concentration Number Items Concentration (mg/L)1 PH 6～9 4 Volatilization powder 0.52 Crude oil 10 5 Sulfide 13Floating objects 100 6Oxygen consumed by Chemicals 100

3) Make full use of waste water. Water in mud pump and water brake must be utilized by

circulating. Water that is used to wash drill floor, shall be recovered after deposition. The

recovery rate of waste water shall reach 85％.

4) Disposed oil shall be recovered entirely. Recovery rate shall be 100％.Dumping of waste oil

shall be prohibited.

5) In drilling operation, non-chrome thinner high temperature mud system shall be used often

if engineering is qualified.

6) While drilling in special area， adopt mud creation inhabitation techniques to decrease the

waste mud creation.

7) After completion, all the mud chemicals, bentonite, clays shall be recovered and shall not

be left at the well site. Mud in purifying tank shall also be recovered after completion.

8) In case of any flammable or noxious gas encountered， steps shall be taken at once to

prevent kicks and blowout. Gas shall be guided into ignition equipment to burn it away.

9) Burning material that may result in serious smog or horrible smell at the well site is not

allowed. For cement or barite that may result in granular dust pollution， operation shall be

performed in a closed system.

10) For abandoned well， filled with cement plugs to seal the oil, gas or water formation.

11) Contractor/Generators are required to transport all generated wastes to the

location/facilities designated, and licensed by authorities and/or approved/verified by the

Company/PEDEC (as appropriate).

12) All wastes shall be transferred by approved Sub‐ Contractor (which could be a 3rd party

Sub-Contractor of the Contractor/Generator itself). The Waste Transporter and any other

waste management service Contractor shall be licensed in accordance with local

regulations and are in compliance with such regulations. Company shall verify and approve

the Waste Transportation Contractor by checking licenses and qualifications, audit and

inspections, verification of human resources and received trainings etc.

13) The transportation Contractor shall ensure that all wastes are transported in a safe and

responsible manner.

4.1 Security for the WORK shall be arranged and administered by the Contractor. CONTRACTOR shall be fully advised in writing covering all aspects of security upon. CONTRACTOR shall liaise with COMPANY QHSE to develop operational and contingency plans prior to Mobilization.

4.2 The Security guards shall be appropriately housed and provided for. Transport for the Security guards to perform field activities shall be made available according to standard Iranian Oilfield procedures.

4.3 All CONTRACTOR’s personnel involved in the WORK shall possess the necessary security clearance documentation required by the Iranian authorities to work within the Operating Area.

Quality

5.1 CONTRACTOR shall manage and operate a Quality Management System (QMS) to ensure that the quality of the WORK performed satisfies the requirements specified under this agreement.

5.2 CONTRACTOR’s QMS shall address the following elements where applicable:

i. Management responsibility.

ii. Quality system.

iii. Contract review.

iv. Document and data control.

v. Product identification and traceability.

vi. Process control.

vii. Inspection and testing.

viii. Inspection, test and measuring equipment.

ix. Control of non-conforming product.

x. Corrective and preventive action.

xi. Handling, storage, packaging, preservation and delivery.

xii. Quality records.

xiii. Internal audit.

xiv. Training.

xv. Servicing.

5.3 CONTRACTOR is responsible for the identification of any deviation from or non-compliance with the CONTRACT. CONTRACTOR shall notify COMPANY of any such deviation or noncompliance and shall not release the affected work without COMPANY permission.

5.4 Subcontractors shall operate QMS’s appropriate to the WORK being performed.

5.5 Where CONTRACTOR employs a subcontractor who cannot demonstrate a formal QMS, he shall submit his proposals for supervision of the subcontractor activities, for COMPANY’S approval.

5.6 CONTRACTOR shall establish suitable controls to ensure that:

 Materials and equipment employed in the performance of the WORK fully comply with the

terms and conditions of the CONTRACT.

 Adequate procedures are in place to ensure that the WORK is performed to the required

standards.

The QHSE plan shall clearly indicate CONTRACTOR’s intentions in this area.

5.7 Maintenance/Inspection/ Audit

CONTRACTOR shall have active and regular maintenance, inspection, audit and cross-audit programs in order to identify and correct defects and deficiencies in equipment, plant, facilities and systems that may affect the timely and efficient completion of the WORK or impact on health, safety and environmental aspects of the WORK. COMPANY reserves the right to be

actively involved in inspection and audit programs and/or conduct its own inspections and audits.

Prior to the start of operations, COMPANY reserves the right to audit CONTRACTOR’s management systems, vehicles, camp facilities, level of training and competence in order to verify compliance with the standards. Operations will not commence without COMPANY acceptance of the CONTRACTOR’s QHSE system and if necessary, agreement of a remedial work /QHSE action item list resulting from the audit.

Meetings

In order to clarify COMPANY’S QHSE expectations of CONTRACTOR and to develop the QHSE plan for the WORK the following meeting shall be held:

6.1 Initial QHSE Management Meeting - attended by COMPANY and CONTRACTOR Representatives and QHSE Managers. During this meeting, the QHSE plan will be discussed, specific action items identified, and responsibilities for follow-up determined. At COMPANY’S discretion this meeting may be made part of the QHSE Planning Meeting.

6.2 QHSE Planning Meeting - attended by CONTRACTOR’s Representative and QHSE Managers,

as proposed. Scheduled after the Initial QHSE Management Meeting and before the start of

operations; at this meeting CONTRACTOR will provide a specific QHSE plan for COMPANY’S

review.

The outcome of the meeting will be a list of action items, time for completion, and responsibilities for completion and acceptance. COMPANY will review the information presented and will respond in a timely manner. Under no circumstances will the WORK start before the specific QHSE plan has been approved by COMPANY.

6.3 Site Start-Up Meeting(s) - attended by all field personnel including subcontractor personnel and COMPANY’S field representatives. The purposes of the meeting(s) will be to:

a) Inform personnel of COMPANY’s and CONTRACTOR’s QHSE requirements and

procedures;

b) Inform personnel of specific HSE hazards, mitigating measures and procedures to

be followed;

c) Inform personnel, including subcontractor supervisors and personnel of their QHSE

responsibilities;

d) Review emergency response and contingency plans.

e)

Well Control

(1) Recording Reference

All BOP tests, drills, function tests, any malfunctions, repair or maintenance to the mud system and well control equipment shall be recorded in the IADC daily reports and shall be signed by both the Drilling Contractor's Tool Pusher and Drilling Supervisor on the well site. They shall also be recorded in the Daily Drilling Report.

(2) Flow Check Procedure

To avoid a kick situation, it is recommended that a flow check be conducted at specific times during the drilling operation.

1) While drilling(any warning sign)

2) Prior to tripping the drill string

3) While tripping(any warning sign)

4) While out of hole(continuously)

5) Trip out:

 After pulling 5% of the drill pipe

 At the midpoint depth of the wellbore

 Prior to pulling the first stand of drill collars

 After all the drill string is out of hole.

6) Trip in:

 Upon reaching surface casing shoe

 At midpoint of the wellbore

(3) Primary Control Reference

It is the responsibility of the Drilling Contractor to keep the hole full at all times by using all the available equipment. The Drilling Supervisor shall monitor that the correct preventive practices are being implemented and/or conducted at all times.

Extreme care shall be taken to monitor mud volume, drilling breaks and gas cut mud.

The MACP (Maximum allowable casing pressure) shall be confirmed by the Drilling Supervisor, either after setting each new casing string or, whenever the density of the drilling mud changes.

The MACP shall be clearly written on a kick control sheet that will be posted near the choke control panel.

Well : AZNS-118 (G04)

Type : IGH

Health, Safety and Environment Page 155 of 175

Development Well

The predetermined distance at which the Tool Joint is to be pulled above the rotary should be posted on rig floor near the BOP control panel.

The Driller is responsible for carefully measuring and recording the slow pump pressure. Awareness of these values is an important element in killing operations, in order to avoid formation breakdown.

Take a measure and record the slow pump pressure at three different flow rates with all pumps at the following times:

 Once per tour.

 Whenever changes occur in the mud density/rheology.

 Whenever changes occur in the dimension and characteristics of the string , BHA, jet

size, jet plugged or jet lost, etc.

(4) Secondary Control Reference

Any time a drilling break is noticed, drilling shall stop and a static monitoring of the well shall be carried out. Space-out for all Pipe Rams and Annular Preventer must be known to the Driller at all times to keep tool joints clear of Annular preventer and rams.

(5) Kick Detection Procedure While Drilling

If there is an indication of a possible kick occurring, the Driller shall follow the kick detection procedures outlined below:

Stop drilling with the pump onPick up top drive to a predetermined space-out positionShut off the pumps and check for well flow by visual examination on the bell nipple or flow line.

1) If the well is not flowing:

 Immediately notify the rig manager, Drilling Supervisor and Toolpusher.

 The Drilling Supervisor will decide whether to circulate bottoms up or resume drilling. The

penetration rate will be limited as long as the cause of the abnormal situation is

undetermined. Always be aware that some hydrocarbons (gas or oil cut mud) may rise to

the surface. Ensure that the proper monitoring and safety systems all in place.

2) If the well is flowing:

 Close the well as per the Shut-in Procedure.

 Immediately notify the Wellsite Drilling Supervisor, rig manager and Toolpusher.

H2S Monitoring and Prevention Measure

9.1 Monitoring H2S

1) Somewhere on the well site is apt to accumulate H2S, such as mud pit, shale shaker, and drilling platform, should be installed one set of. Make sure H2S monitor and alarm system can be used.

2) Alarm threshold setting for H2S warning indicator: the first warning level is 10mg/m3, unable warning sound, just show present value of H2S content to operators. The second warning level is 20mg/m3, when the H2S content in air exceed 20mg/m3, H2S warning indicator can give an alarm automatically, and warning sound should be heard by everyone work in well site. The audible alarm should be installed on Kelly board.

3) Equip enough gas masks on drilling platform, shaker screen (total amount should large than 24), and equip 12 sets of 30min positive pressure Air Aspirators， 2 sets of portable H2S monitor and appropriative H2S warning indicator. Arrange a person specially assigned for taking charge all the protective apparatus. This man must do periodical check to keep these equipment in a good condition.

Well : AZNS-118 (G04)

Type : IGH

Health, Safety and Environment Page 156 of 175

Development Well

4) H2S monitor should be done weekly check and compulsory examination.

5) After drilling into the pay zone, strengthen the detection of H2S content in mud.

6) Contractor shall implement H2S service system by himself or 3rd party for controlling the risk of H2S leakage.

9.2 Measures for H2S prevention

1) Windsocks shall be set on the drill floor and in the monsoon direction or/and prevailing wind direction of Azadegan oil field, off well site. In emergency, employees must follow the evacuation procedure.

2) Explosion-proof blowers shall be installed around the rig, shale shaker, and drilling fluid tanks to blow away toxic and flammable gas at the well site.

3) The rig crew in the H2S area shall be furnished with H2S monitors and adequate protective apparatus in accordance with IPS. Everybody shall be able to use, maintain, and check the apparatus.

4) The technicians of rig crew are responsible for H2S proof training and the tool pusher is in charge of the supervision and examination.

5) The pH value of drilling fluid in the H2S area shall be kept over 9.5. Pay attention to measurement of H2S concentration in drilling fluid. The H2S concentration shall be kept at less than 50mg/cm3 by using sulphur removal agents and degassers.

6) Personnel safety and protection shall be carried out according to IPS during operations in polluted areas where H2S content exceeds the critical concentration.

7) When the rig crew makes decision to bleed and fire because well control cannot be performed due to well site conditions, operations shall be performed according to procedures in IPS.

8) Once blowout occurs, the incident shall be reported to the higher level department as soon as possible and fire fighting trucks, ambulances, medical care personnel and technicians shall be on duty at the well site.

9) After the blowout is controlled, H2S concentration should be measured in the places where H2S is possible to gather.

10) The personnel are not allowed to access until the H2S concentration is below the critical concentration.

(1) to ensure all H2S equipment is in good condition,

(2) to supply, maintain, inspect, calibrate and repair all H2S equipment to the highest standard and have this

equipment ready for use all the time,

(3) to supply the gas detectors &amp;amp; required accessories to automate the gas detection program with capabilities

of continually evaluate the condition of the gas monitor and should be able to collect information and

provide automate &amp;amp; manual alerts regarding the status of the available toxics gases,

(4) to review COMPANY’s H2S evacuation procedure and to advise where change/modifications are required,

(5) to monitor levels of H2S, inform COMPANY, and take any necessary precautions to safeguard life, the

well and the Equipment from impending danger,

(6) to customise safety training program,

(7) to maintain a minimum inventory of parts and accessories to facilitate ease and quality provision of service,

(8) to prepare and submit detailed technical and quality reports on timely basis.

(9) To keep to be available at rig site on 24 hours/day.

the drilling, complete with dual level (10-20ppm) visual and audible alarms at different points of the rig

site, to allow for early warning of all personnel on the rig for H2S gas occurrence in the atmosphere, hence

ease of following emergency plans to safety briefing areas. All equipment and devices shall be on location

before well SPUND-IN, service personnel shall be on location before spud in the hole section which contain

(11) The supply of a comprehensive cascade breathing air loop system, complete with breathing air supply

compressors, storage cylinders, and work/escape and rescue breathing apparatus. This system shall provide

at time of gas detection and alarm for essential personnel, who will be responsible to control the well during

emergencies, an extended period of time under fresh breathing air, to allow for non-essential personnel to

escape to safe briefing areas, and for rescue teams to rescue any injured persons during H2S emergencies.

(12) The safety supervision performed by qualified personnel at rig site, to install, commission and maintains in

Safety Plan.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 2

2. Location and Environment Information

The well S377 is located at middle part in South Azadegan Oil Field, Iran. This well is N348 ºW of AZN-17 and 68m far. The average monthly temperature is 45 oC in summer, and highest temperature is 52 oC; the lowest temperature is -4.4 oC in winter. Maximum rainfall is 78 mm in February, winter season.

3.1 General Formation Lithology Descriptions

1) In a typical geological section, one massive oolitic to pellety limestone with minor contemporaneous

brecciation in the basal part developed in Fahliyan Formation (James and Wynd, 1965). The Fahliyan

Formation has been drilled in Well AZN-1, AZN-2, AZN-4, AZN-5 and AZN-6 and the Fahliyan

Formation in Well AZN-1, AZN-2 and AZN-5 are mainly consisted of poorly fossiliferous, generally

micritic limestone, often argillaceous, and marls with no porosity butextensive and thick porous

micritic and chalky limestones were developed in Well AZN-4 and AZN-6.

2) In the Gadvan Formation, a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales

and sandstones were deposited in various environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is

composed of low-energy, but not very deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone with echinoids and

algal fragments. Two sandstone units were defined in the Gadvan Formation， lateral changes and

especially discontinuity of the sandstone layers which are consist of fine clastics (shales and

siltstones) may be expected.

3) The thicknees of Khalij Member is about 14m in the Azadegan Field, and main lightology is

limestone, containing algal and echinoid debris. Although fair porosity values were observed, the

limestone was very tight and has a very low permeability.The Dariyan Formation in the Azadegan

Field developed grey to brown thick-bedded limestone which is rich in Orbitorina.

4) In the Azadegan Field, the upper part of the Kazhdumi Formation is made up of dark-grey to

dark-brown marls, the middle part developed light-grey, micritic to chalky limestone, the low part, is

around40-60m in thichnessclean channel sands interbeded argillaceous sands with streaks of shale.

5) The Sarvak Formation developed a carbonate unit which was gradually overlying the Kazhdumi

Formation, and it was abruptly terminated by an erosion phase and covered by various late

Cretaceous sediments. A Typical geological section of the Sarvak Formation in Azadegan reveals

that dark grey fine grained nodulary argillaceous limestone interbedded with thin marls in the bottom,

and graded upward into a white-to-buff massive chalky limestone with siliceous nodules;it was

developed a very massive tan-to-brown limestone containing abundant rudist debris in the middle

part; the upper part is made up of rubbly limestone with red staining and leached zones of breccia

and the top of Sarvak Formation is very ferruginous.

6) The Laffan Formation developed a thin greenish-grey pyritic shale layer, capping the Sarvak

Formation. It was encountered in the Azadegan wells and only ostracods were found. It is a cap rock

for the upper part of the Sarvak reservoir.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 5

7) In the Azadegan Field, the Ilam Formation consists of grey micritic limestone, often argillaceous,

rarely pyritic, limestones. Microfauna, mostly pelagic, including rotalids and oligostegina

assemblage, a few algae, bryozoa, and rudist fragments exist.

8) The Gurpi Formation in the Azadegan Field consists of grey to dark-brownish, slightly argillaceous

micritic limestone with Maestrichtian to Campanian aged fauna. At Well AZN-1, about 50 meters of

chalky limestones were encountered in the middle of the Gurpi formation, which contains abundant

fragments of rudists, bryozoa, corals, and Orbitoides.

9) Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field. The upper part is made of grey to

dark-brown argillaceous limestones and marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in

Oligocene. The lower part is composed of dark-brown, slightly argillaceous limestone. Pelagic fauna,

mostly Globigerina and Globorotalia, indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to late

Eocene in age. It seems that no early Paleocene Formation exists in the Azadegan area.

10) The Asmari Formation in Azadegan Field developed a massive sandstone interval, with a few thin

argillaceous layers in the upper sections. Sands are clear, fine to medium-sorted, and poorly

consolidated. On the other hand, the lower sections consist of intercalation of sandy, chalky, slightly

dolomitic high-energy limestone with sands layers.

11) The Gachsaran Formation with a thickness ranges from 400 m to 600 m, thicken towards the north.

Deposition of the Gachsaran Formation is cyclic. The cycle began with a deposition of shallow-water

marine carbonates, and then it was overlain by a thick evaporitic phase. The evaporites were capped

by red to variegated marl, which represented the end of cycle.

12) The AghaJari Formation consists of a repetition of brown to grey calcareous sandstones, chert debris

and gypsum-veined red marl. A fauna of brackish to freshwater was observed, together with more

marine fauna.

3.2 Key Reservoir Descriptions

There are three key reservoirs that we need to focus on. Gross thickness of Sarvak-3 ranges from 16m to 30m and averaged at 21.4m.The average thickness of net-pay zone in Sarvak-3 is 15.8m and the Net/Gross ratio 1s 79% .It is mainly composed of light brown or dark brown bituminous dolomitic limestone, which is dominated by bioturbated and bioclastic packstones and grainstone. It is a main oil-bearing zone with a better petrophysical property with respect to others of Sarvak Formation in the south azadegan. The mean porosity is 16.8% and the mean permeability is 20.7 md and So is 89.1%. The forecasted top depth is -2752m in S377.

4. Formation or Pore Pressure and Temperature

4.1 Formation or Pore Pressure Prediction

According to the statistic data of drilled wells in Azadegan oil field, formation pressure forecast is done mainly based on the off-set wells testing in this field, especially based on the DST testing results and RFT data in Sarvak formations.

It is known that there exist two sets of different formation pressure system from north to south blocks as the boundary by AZN-10 in this oil field according to drilling fluid density, logging data and formation pressure analysis results from existing wells. In North of AZN-10(Figure3a), there are two

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 6

abnormal pressure formations, one in Gachsaran (1330~1950m) with the pore pressure coefficient 1.6~2.0, and the other in Fahliyan reservoir formation with the pore pressure coefficient around 1.6~1.3 (F2:1.6, F4:1.3). In South of AZN 10(Figure 3b), the formation pore pressure of Gachsaran tends to become normal pressure system, but the formation pore pressure of Fahliyan has also a pore pressure coefficient around 1.6~1.3 (F2:1.6, F4:1.3).

All the other reservoirs such as Gadvan, Kazhdumi and Sarvak formations exists in normal pressure systems, with pore pressure coefficient between 1.1 and 1.20. Caving pressure coefficient is lower than that of pore pressure in some formations, and the fracture pressure coefficient is between 1.8 and 2.2. The formation pressure forecasting curve is shown in below. Well S377 belongs to Area II.

FPS of North Block FPS of South Block

These estimations can only be used as a rough reference for the future job because of the existence of uncertainty in down-hole situation and lack of reliable data.Well S377 belongs to South Block.

6. Drilling History and Lessions learnt

6.1 Drilling History

The Azadegan Oil field was discovered when Well AZN-1 was drilled in 1999 whose total depth is 4643m at the Cretaceous formation.

Before CNPCI (Iran), 40 wells (7 exploration wells, 2 appraisal well and 31 producing wells) have been drilled in the Azadegan Field, among which 23 wells are located in the South Azadegan Contract Area, 5 wells (AZNS-026, AZNS-027, AZNS-028, AZNS-029 &amp;amp; AZNS-030) has been drilled by CNPCI (Iran) and 2 well (AZNS-031 &amp;amp; AZNS-032) are currently being drilled.

Carbonate reservoir of Sarvak in the Cretaceous formation is broadly developed in Azadegan Field and was penetrated in 6 exploration wells (AZN-1, AZN-2, AZN-3, AZN-4, AZN-5 and AZN-6). Classic oil bearing zones of Kazhdumi Formation developed in main anticline of Azedegan Field from north to center of South Azadegan. Classic oil bearing zones of Gadvan Formation developed only in crest area and south parts of South Azadegan Filed, which was discovered by AZN-1 in crest and AZN-4 in south parts.

6.2 Lesson learned

Well AZNS-17H:

The 17 ½” hole section had been drilled to 1140m with 70-82 PCF W.B.M, the formation drilled is Aghajari-Gachsaran, tight hole (1140-50m) was encounter when during the condition trip, wash and ream several times this interval. Totally operation is smoothly, a proper mud weight in this section is important for the well’s operations.

The 12 ¼”hole is drilled to 1479 m with 85-88 PCF W.B.M, the drilled formation is Gachsaran. Tight hole was encountered during the condition trip, wash and ream several times this interval and increase MW f/88 to 100 pcf. Totally operation is smoothly, a proper mud weight in this section is important for the well’s operations.

While drilled 17 ½” and 12 ¼” hole section in Aghajari and Gachsaran formation observed bit and stabilizer balled up.

The best approach is to use good fundamentals: an inhibitive mud, optimal hydraulics and a quality ROP enhancer.

Cure bit balling

• Work the bit by rotating fast off bottom and reciprocating the string to shake off the clay coating.

• Reduce ROP for a short time.

• Spot a walnut pill.

• Circulation.

• Fresh water.

• If bit balling is observed while drilling, a detergent is usually added to disperse the balled clays.

• The use of a small percentage of glycol also helps to reduce bit balling.

• Consider fresh water pill.

• Keep the API fluid loss as low as possible.

The 8 ½” hole is drilled to 1970m with 75 pcf salt water w/2-12 BPH losses. For the mud losses, a mud system with LCM is required.

The 6⅛” hole was drilled to 3595.5m MD with 76 PCF O.B.M, the formation drilled was Gachsaran-Asmari-Pabdeh-Ilam-Sarvak-Kazhdumi, the main problem is stuck pipe @ 3595.5m , MD in Kazhdumi Fm. work on stuck with over pull, pipe got free and continue drilled to TD with 73 PCF O.B.M so a proper mud weight in this section is important for the well’s operations.

1.1 Design Basis

This design basis of drilling program is as follows:

1) Geological Proposal of S377 on Pad 50

2) The Iranian Petroleum Standards (IPS) and Iranian Laws and Regulations

3) SY 5333-1996, “Drilling Engineering Design Standard”

4) Revised Master Development Plan of Azadegan oil field

5) Front End Engineering Design (FEED) for South Azadegan Oilfield in Iran

6) Drilling history of AZN-17H.

4. Requirement of Drilling and completion Fluid

4.1 Formation or Pore Pressure and Mud Weight

Primary Well Control relies on the hydrostatic pressure of drilling fluid to balance the formation pore pressure. In order to counteract any minor pressure reductions on bottom due to swabbing

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 20

effect when tripping, it is customary to maintain a hydrostatic column with a pressure slightly exceeding the pore pressure of the exposed formation. This overbalance, safety margin, or trip margin, Ptm, varies depending on circumstances but it is generally of the order of 200 psi for conventional operations. The required density Pf to control a formation at a depth, D, with a pressure, Po, is given by: Pf = (Po+Ptm), or Df = (Po+Ptm)/D

To gas cut or contaminated mud, Df = Do + (0.07-0.15) sg,

To oil and/or salt water contaminated mud, Df = Do + (0.05-0.10) sg;

4.2 Requirement of Solid Control

1) Each kind of solid control equipment should be designed to process 100% to 150% of the maximum

anticipated circulating volume of mud. Shale shaker, de-sander, de-silter and cleaner are required to

run all the time during drilling and centrifuge is recommended to run at least 80% of drilling time.

2) The sand content and MBT should be kept as low as possible by solid removal and dilution.

Batchwise dilution (replacement of 20-30% of the total drilling fluid volume in one circulation) is

more effective than continuous dilution.

3) When drilling 12-1/4″ hole section the centrifuges should be run in barite recovery mode to minimize

waste volumes generated. Two centrifuges are recommended for processing salt-saturate mud to

maintain mud weight.

4) The detailed requirement of solid control equipment are as follows:

4.3 Guideline for Mud Program

1) This is a preliminary program. The contractor should provide Drilling Fluid Program based on this

basic proposal.

2) The lowest possible PV is essential for: low frictional losses &amp;amp; optimal hole cleaning.

3) The Yield Point (YP) is calculated by subtracting the PV from the Fan reading at 300 rpm, giving a

result in lbs/100ft2. YP = 2xR300 - R600. An optimal YP is essential for: carry capacity of drilling

fluid (a rule of thumb is that YP = 0.75 x hole size in inches); stability of drilling fluid towards

settling of solids (a rule of thumb is that YP = 9 x specific gravity) &amp;amp; the hole capacity of the drilling

fluid.

4) Optimally the 10’ gel value is 1.5 x the 10” gel value.

5) Always maintain the readings of 6 rpm between 1.2 and 1.5 times of hole size in inches.

6) Drill from Asmari to Sarvak, Formation, KCl polymer mud will be mixed for this section. Losses are

common (even with the light mud weights used) in the Asmari, Sarvak formations. Sized CaCO3

materials are used as lost circulation material. Mud weight shall be kept as low as possible. High pH

shall be maintained to control any small amounts of H2S and CO2 gas. In the Sarvak Wells, lubricant

may be required as the hole dictates. KCl Polymer mud will be transferred to CaCO3 based Drill-In

fluid to drill the Sarvak reservoir. Mud weight shall be kept as low as possible. CaCO3 is the optimal

weighting material and barite is back up as required.

4.4 Kill Fluid and Chemical Storage

1) The kill fluid should be prepared prior to commence of each hole section. The density of kill fluid is

usually heavier than the formation coefficient by 0.4sg and the volume of kill fluid is following the

regulation of South Azadegan Oil Field:

For formation pore pressure coefficient more than 1.20 and pay zone without H2S, the volume of kill mud is 0.5 times of full hole volume;

For formation pore pressure coefficient more than 1.20 and pay zone with H2S, the volume of kill mud is 1.5 times of full hole volume;

2) The following emergency or contingency materials should be kept on the rig site at all times to

supplement the proposed mud system. Kick, lost circulation, stuck pipe scenarios, lubrication and

general purpose operations may require the following products to be on storage or standby.

4.5 Requirement of Completion Fluid

1) As the base case, completion fluid for all production wells will be KCl and CaCl2 clear brine with

1.19-1.36 g/cm3 density according to the formation pressure (Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Fahliyan, Gadvan

and Asmari) and due to requirements of well control density of completion fluid should be 0.05-0.10

g/cm3 above formation coefficient.

2) The packer fluid is to be KCl brine with corrosion inhibitor, oxygen scavenger, bactericide, etc for

preserving tubular.

3) The performance requirements designed for the completion fluid system is as below:

Particle Plugging Test and Recovered permeability: ≥90%; or NTU: ≤30; Corrosion rate: ＜ 0.076 mm/a;

4) Cartridge filters must be used and diameter of solid particle: ≤2μ m.

5) PH shall be kept in the range of 9.5-10

5.2 Preparation for Running Casing

1) Prior to running casing wiper trip should be conducted and any tight spots should be reamed to

ensure borehole in good condition. Especially, for 8-1/2″ hole section the wiper trip BHA with two

stabilizers is highly recommended please refer to 6.2 BHA.

2) Circulate to condition mud to ensure hole static, no loss and no gain.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 24

3) Change BOP ram to casing size and test same to the pressure equal to the value of 70-80% casing

burst with fresh water.

4) Cross check the casing tally, casing accessories such as, floating equipment, centralizer, stop collar

and pin, drift rabbit, cement head, crossover for cement head, cement plugs, circulation swage, thread

dope, thread lock compound, etc.

5) Double check the casing running equipment and tools according to the detail list.

6) Pilot lab test for slurry and contamination test with different ratio of mud and slurry have been done

and the measures are considered in cement program as per the result of pilot test.

7) Ensure cement and additives available on site and keep silo, cement unit, water tank, surface high

pressure lines and manifold in good condition.

8) The cement program should be submitted to review and approve by drilling superintendent 24 hrs

before cementing job.

5.3 Guideline for Cement Program

1) Overall design philosophy is to achieve simplicity as far as possible while keeping the high operation

quality for all wells.

2) As bottom of hole static temperatures exceed 230℉ , or 110℃, 35% of silica flour will be used in

cementing composition to maintain low permeability and help prevent loss of compressive strength

of cement sheath.

3) Cement slurry of high density is required for ultra-high pressure wells. Heavyweight materials such

as hematite should be prepared for increasing cement slurry density.

4) Cement additives for improving elastic properties of cement rock could be used for cementing the

hole with slim annulus clearance.

5) Rotating Liner hanger with integral top packer and rotating spiral centralizers will be used.

6) The other guideline is listed in the table as follows. And refer to the detail cement program,

Attachment-2. This should be prepared by Contractor, and return to Company at least 2 weeks

before spud in.

7) After cementing job, it is required to close BOP and apply back pressure on annulus to wait on tail

slurry thickening. The back pressure is depending on the density of tail slurry and at annulus in

VERTICAL HEIGHT. The formula is: Back Pressure = (Dts-1.07sg)*Htvd*(0.0098*145 ) (psi);

e.g., after cement 9-5/8″ casing, Back Pressure = (1.94-1.07sg)*550m*(0.0098*145) = 680 (psi).

6.2 BHA Proposal

1) 26” hole section drilling BHA:

Bit + float sub w/ TOTCO ring + 9-1/2” (or 9”) DC x 2 (or 3) joints + 26” stabilizer + 9-1/2” (or 9”) DC x 4 joints + X/O + 8” DC x 6 joints + X/O + 5” HWDP to surface.

2) 17-1/2” hole section drilling BHA:

Bit + float sub w/ TOTCO ring + 9-1/2 (or 9”) NMDC + 9-1/2” (or 9”) DC x 1 (or 2) joint + 17-1/2” stabilizer + 9-1/2” (or 9”) DC x 3 joints + X/O + 8” DC x 9 joints + 8” drilling jar + X/O + 6-1/2” (or 6-1/4”) DC x 3 joints + 5” HWDP x 15 joints.

Note:9-1/2” shock absorber sub should be available on site in case of bit bouncing during drilling top section hole.

3) 12-1/4” hole section drilling BHA:

Bit + float sub w/ TOTCO ring + 8” NMDC + 8” DC x 1 joint + 12-1/8” stabilizer + 8” DC x 12 joints + 8” drilling jar + X/O + 6-1/2” (or 6-1/4”) DC x 3 joints + (X/O) + 5” HWDP x 15 joints + 5” DP

4) 12-1/4” hole section directional drilling BHA:

5) 8-1/2” hole section horizontal drilling BHA:

6) Recommended wiper trip BHA for running 5 -1/2” liner:

Bit + bit sub w/ float valve + 6-1/2” (or 6-1/4”) DC x 2 joint + 8-3/8” stabilizer + 6-1/2” (or 6-1/4”) DC x 1 joint + 8-3/8” stabilizer + 6-1/2” (or 6-1/4”) DC x 14 joints + 6-1/2” (or 6-1/4”) drilling jar + (X/O) + 5” HWDP x 15 joints + 5” DP

7. Directional Drilling

7.1 Anti-collision measures

Anti-collision should be considered if more than one wells on the same pad. The distance between every two cellars is only 15m. The risk of collision between two slots likely occurs in vertical section. Anti-collision issue of long vertical section is a big challenge and anti-collision measures are as following:

1) From surface to kick off point: In upper formation, adopt pendulum BHA and low WOB and high

RPM to guarantee drilling upper hole vertically.

2) Single-shot and Multi-shot will be used to monitor and survey the well trajectory. Take survey of

single-shot every 400-500 m, if the deviation is nearly or larger than required, then survey should be

taken more frequently and well straightening measure should be performed. The multi-shot survey

should be taken during the last trip and the survey interval of multi-shot is not more than 30m.

3) After survey, the drilled well path should be compared to existing and other proposed wells. The

distances between other wells and the drilled well are calculated at frequent depth intervals in the

critical section. These calculations can be performed using the application COMPASS, WELLPLAN

or NAVIGATOR. Survey uncertainty must also be computed both for the drilled well and the critical

existing wells.

4) Steering BHA (Motor and MWD) should be back-up. Run Steering BHA (Motor and MWD)

whenever potential collision risk increased.

5) Existing well survey data

The well AZN-17 is the nearest offset well, which is 68m far away from this well, anti-collision scan is required with the survey data I in the detailed directional drilling program.

9.4 Kill and Choke Manifold Sketch

Note: The minimum OD(outside diameter) of the burning line (line from Poor boy degasser to burning pit) shall be 8”. The minimum ID （ nominal inside diameter） for bleed off line (from choke manifold to burning pit) shall be 3” (76.2mm) for 10K, 15K and 20K arrangements. Both the burning line and bleed off line shall be installed with flanged type connections, no welding is allowed.

9.5 Pressure Test

1) Accumulator, BOP, Wellhead and Choke/Kill Manifold

 The BOP, wellhead, choke manifold, and related equipment are normally to be tested on 14 days

basis. This period can be extended depending on the well operations and the specifications in the

Drilling Program and the applicable Regulations.

 The accumulator is to be tested prior to the first test of the BOP, or any time after repairs are

made to the accumulator unit. The accumulator bottles pre-charge pressure shall be checked

before drilling out the casing shoe or every 4 weeks whichever is earlier.

 All the pressure tests shall be performed with both low pressure test, usually 300 psi and high

pressure test, usually to the rated value (for annular BOP is 70% of WP) or as per Drilling

Program. All high pressure tests should be maintained for 10-30 minutes, and low pressure test 5

minutes, unless the Drilling Program dictates otherwise.

2) Casing and Liner

In the below table, casing or liner integrity test pressure, surface SIP, is given when test medium is fresh water. As usual, green test casing integrity after bump plug is recommended in the cement procedure. If rigs use the displacement mud (density &amp;gt;1.0sg) to test casing, the pressure applied on surface cement head should be considered the mud hydrostatic pressure at casing collar! And the surface SIP is given as the following formula:

Surface shut-in Pressure = 80% Casing Burst – B (psi)

B=Drilling fluid hydrostatic pressure - fresh water hydrostatic pressure;

Notes: the assumption in the formula above is the external fluid of the casing is fresh water(density:1.01sg), in order to simply the calculation the test fluid inside casing is also assumed as fresh water(density:1.01sg), the actual test pressure at well site need to be recalculated and are subject to the real mud weight in hole.

3) Wellhead

 For port seal: Install a test pump to the port and inject test fluid and test pressure to which is less

than 80% of casing collapse. Hold and monitor the test pressure for 15 minutes. In the event of a

leak, remove the Wellhead and replace the seals as required. Once a satisfactory test is achieved,

carefully bleed off all test pressure, drain the test fluid and reinstall the dust cap.

 For flange seal: Flange seal and body integrity test will be conducted at the same time when

pressure test for ram BOP together.

4) LOT/FIT

 LOT will be performed in the first well in every area and in all appraisal wells to determine the

equivalent mud weight at which the formation will take fluid. LOT will be conducted after drill

out 20″, 13-3/8″, 9-5/8″ and 7″ shoe track to the first weak zone, especially the sand zone, but no

more than 20 m in the new section hole.

 FIT will be performed in the other wells. The test is not designed to breakdown or fracture

formation, just to test the capacity of formation strength. Shut in pressure in wellhead during FIT

should be the value that the hydrostatic pressure at casing shoe equals to max mud weight that

will be used plus 0.2sg, or 12.5pcf. EMW (sg) = MWmax (sg) + 0.2 sg; or EMW (pcf) = MWmax

(pcf) + 12.5 pcf.

Note: As S377 is the first well to be drilled in area 2 by CNPCI, it is required to perform LOT.

9.6 Well Control during Special operation

The well control while wireline logging and running casing is important and critical, best practices shall be followed, including but not limited to:

1) Change BOP ram to casing size and pressure test same to 1500 psi of surface SIP or the value of 80%

casing burst with fresh water.

2) The crossover for casing connection to drill pipe must available on floor. A good practice is to keep a

joint of DP with casing crossover at lower end and full opened safety valve at upper end to be ready

for picking up on V-door.

3) Prior to wireline logging, install shooting nipple and pressure test same to again WL BOP to 1500

psi.

4) The cable cutter should be available on rig floor. If kick occurs, cut logging cable and to close blend

BOP is optional

5) The contractor shall perform relative pressure test, drills, safety checks etc as per detailed well

control procedure of CNPC during the well operations.
