10. Drilling Operation Procedure

10.1 Pre-spud Preparation

1) Certificate

 Conduct rig accept inspection to make sure that the rig is as per Drilling Contract, all rig

equipment is on location, properly rigged up and function-tested. Check availability and content

of hoisting equipment inspection &amp;amp; maintenance records according to API.

 Make sure that all HSE procedures and systems are in place according to the HSE specifications,

and that all personnel has been made aware of relevant HSE guidelines and procedures. Check

certification of rig crane and rig crane operator; Check validity of well control certificates for key

contractor personnel, e.g. Tool pusher, drillers, assistant drillers.

2) Safety Device

 Test draw-works crown block saver and record results on daily drilling report; test derrick man

escape device (Geronimo line) and record time required for test weight on daily drilling report;

test rig intercom system, especially for Driller-Derrick Man and Driller-Mud Logger

communication link.

 Check that all rig safety equipment, e.g. fire extinguishers, eye-wash stations etc., is installed at

the proper locations as per rig layout diagrams.

 Verify that substructure is level and foundation adequate to support drilling unit during whole

well period and that mast is centered above rotary table.

 Ensure rig alignment with Top Drive System also. Make sure rig tongs and rig floor hoisting

equipment are adequate for handling 8″ and 9-1/2″ drill collars. Make sure that mechanical tong

torque gauges for drill pipe/drill collar are properly functioning and have been calibrated;

re-calibrate as required.

3) Rig Equipment

 Check condition of contractor’s tubular to be utilized during the 26″ section, including all subs,

X/O, DC and HWDP, on conformity with the drilling contract’s specifications. Verify that the 26″

tri-cone bit is ready to be picked up, all nozzles installed, and 26″ bit breakers are on the rig floor.

Make sure single-shot or multi-shot inclinator available for vertical section survey.

 Make sure all solids control equipment are correctly installed and have been function-tested with

charging pressure gauges in place where applicable. Make sure adequate screen inventory for the

shale shakers is available in the sizes required for the well. Make sure water tanks are completely

filled and transfer lines from water tanks to mud system are hooked up. Make sure all mud

materials and chemicals required to drill 26″ section are on location, in sound condition and

suitably stored. Make sure that bentonite is pre-hydrated and sufficient volume has been built for

top section and for well-kill.

 Make sure that all mud pumps have been fitted with new consumable parts: liners, pistons,

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 44

valves, seats etc. Make sure that pulsation dampeners are charged with Nitrogen to the proper

pre-charge pressure, 30% of expected standpipe pressure during drilling of the 26″ hole section;

check if sufficient Nitrogen bottles are available on location to adjust pulsation dampener

pre-charge pressure as required. Make sure that mud pump pop-off valves are set at the proper

pressure for the liner sizes installed and that the mud discharge lines from the pop-off valves to

the mud tanks are properly secured. Check that all high-pressure hoses are properly secured with

safety chains. P/T should be done to high-pressure surface line of circulating system.

4) Wellhead &amp;amp; BOP

 Make sure that BOP handling system is working. Have 2″ line pipe nipple with 2″ ball valve

welded to 42″ conductor pipe 0.2m above cellar bottom to drain cement from conductor/riser

pipe after cementing 20″ surface casing. Verify that pre-installed 42″ conductor pipe is vertical

and that no angle/bend exists between conductor and 42″ riser pipe.

 Make sure that adequate supply of 20″ casing is on location, inspected and ready to be run, float

shoe should already be made up, centralizers should be pre-installed on the pipe-rack prior to

spud in the well.

 Ensure mud logging unit is functioning and equipment are calibrated and tested properly.

5) Others

 Verify that the rig’s fuel tanks are full to meet minimum of three days rig consumption available

on site.

 Make sure all waste treatment equipment available and commissioning test is ok.

 Hold pre-spud meeting with relevant service Company personnel on location. Discuss procedures

involved throughout 26″ hole section drilling, reporting system and critical safety operation

measures.

10.2 Drill 26” Hole

10.2.1 Drill 26″ hole to section TD

1) Tag the bottom of cellar and control ROP to start drilling. Drill with pre-hydrated bentonite mud the

first and second stands at 60-70 RPM with 400 GPM of low flow rate to avoid washing out the

conductor.

2) Pump Hi-Vis pill to sweep hole when every stand drilled down. Continue drilling to section TD with

normal parameters.

3) Circulate hole clean. POOH to surface. Check bit if balling or not. Make condition trip. Spot Hi-Vis

pill after circulate hole clean. Drop Totco survey, POOH and retrieve Totco.

4) Hold pre-job safety meeting with involve personnel for running 20″ casing.

5) Rig up and function test 20″ casing running equipment.

6) Match the stab-in sub with float shoe on surface.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 45

10.2.2 Run 20″ casing

1) Run 20″ casing as per casing tally.

2) Lock shoe track connections. Fill casing with weighted mud every joint to keep weight balance

between inside casing and annular. Monitor WOH during running casing to detect excessive drag.

3) Rig up C-plate. Run stab-in sub (with new O-ring seal), 5″ pup joint (with 5″ x 20″ bow spring

centralizer) and 5″ HWDP as stinger string in 20″ casing

4) Slowly lower stinger string and make sure stab into float shoe ok.

5) Make up circulating head. Fill up annulus (5″ x 20″) with weighted mud and check the stinger seal.

6) Secure the inner string to the rig substructure with slings.

7) Break circulation slowly. And circulate 200% of annular volume with mud at 800-1000 GPM.

10.2.3 Cement 20″ casing

1) Cement 20″ casing as cement program.

2) P/U stinger, circulate to flush string inside. POOH.

3) If no slurry returns to surface, top-fill job should be performed.

4) WOC &amp;amp; L/D 26″ BHA.

10.2.4 Install Wellhead Section A

1) Install Wellhead Section A as per wellhead engineer.

2) Correctly determine elevation from top flange of CHH to RKB and then cut CP &amp;amp; 20″ casing.

3) Install wellhead section A, 20″ SOL x 20-3/4″ 3K Casing Head Housing following the right

direction.

4) Pressure test CHH port seal and refer to 9.5 Pressure Test.

5) N/U Spacer, kill/choke manifold and 20-3/4″ 3K BOP stack.

6) P/T wellhead &amp;amp; BOP system as required.

7) Set wear bushing.

10.3 Drill 17-1/2” Hole

10.3.1 Drill 17-1/2″ hole to section TD

1) M/U and RIH with 17-1/2″ drilling BHA.

2) Tag TOC. Circulate.

3) P/T casing integrity as per 9.5: Pressure Test.

4) Displace new mud into hole.

5) Drill out shoe track with reduced parameters.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 46

6) Continue drilling to the first weakest zone, especially the sand zone. New hole is not more than 20m.

7) Circulate hole clean. POOH into casing shoe.

8) Perform LOT as procedure.

9) Drill with reduced parameters before stabilizer in casing shoe.

10) Drill to section TD, with optimized parameter.

11) Take survey following 7.1 anti-collision measures.

12) Wiper trip every 300m or depending on hole condition.

13) Drill hole to TD, wiper trip, circulate, run multi-shot survey. Perform flow check before POOH and

bit enter the casing shoe.

10.3.2 Wire-line logging

1) Perform wire-line log for GR/CDR.

2) Perform condition trip for running casing.

10.3.3 Run 13-3/8″ Casing

1) Retrieve wear bushing;

2) R/U casing running tools;

3) Run 13-3/8″ casing and install centralizers as per tally;

4) Function test floating equipment.

5) Thread-lock all shoe track connections. Function test float equipment.

6) Fill up casing every joint with fill-up tool. Monitor hook loads during running of the casing to detect

excessive drag.

7) Tag bottom to confirm casing setting depth.

8) Rig up cementing head, connect cement line and pressure test same

9) Circulate at least one bottom up or till shale shaker clean.

10.3.4 Cement 13-3/8″ casing

1) Hook up cement line and cement pump, pressure test with same.

2) Hold safety meeting with related persons

3) Perform cement 13-3/8″ casing as cement program.

4) If no slurry returns to surface, top-fill job should be considered.

5) WOC.

10.3.5 Install Wellhead Section B

1) Set 13-3/8″ casing hanger in CHH with required tension.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 47

2) L/D cementing head &amp;amp; landing joint.

3) N/D 20-3/4″ BOP and Spacer Spool.

4) N/U 20-3/4″ 3k x 13-5/8″ 5k casing spool, and P/T port seal.

5) N/U BOP stack.

6) P/T BOP system and casing spool as per 9.5: Pressure Test.

7) Retrieve test plug and install wear bushing.

10.4 Drill 12-1/4” Vertical Hole

10.4.1 Drill 12-1/4″ Hole to KOP

1) M/U &amp;amp; RIH 12-1/4″ BHA and tag TOC.

2) Drill Cement plug till 7m above the 13-3/8” shoe, Circulate a bottoms up.

3) Pressure test 13-3/8″ casing integrity as per 9.5: Pressure Test.

4) Displace new mud into hole.

5) Cont. drill out the shoe track and drill the new formation to the first weakest zone, especially the sand

zone, but new hole is not more than 20m.

6) Circulate hole clean. POOH into casing shoe.

7) Perform LOT as procedure and note it in DDR.

8) Drill ahead with reduced parameters till stabilizer has passed through casing shoe.

9) Drill to KOP with optimized parameter.

10) Take survey following 7.1 anti-collision measures.

11) Wiper trip every 300m or depending on hole condition.

12) Drill to KOP depth, perform wiper trip, circulate, drop .multi-shot survey tool. POOH. Perform flow

check before POOH and entering the casing shoe.

10.4.2 Wireline logging

Perform wireline logging: GR/CDR/CALI

10.5 Drill 12-1/4” Directional Hole

10.5.1 Drill 12-1/4” Directional Hole

1) Install wear bushing.

2) M/U 12-1/4" directional BHA and function test same on surface.

3) RIH W/12-1/4" directional BHA to KOP and drill to section TD with optimized parameters.

4) Wiper trip will be performed once every 300m footages drilled or depending on hole condition, take

survey as required.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 48

5) Circulate bottoms up. Wiper trip to 13-3/8″ casing shoe. RIH to bottom and wipe out any tight spots.

Circulate hole clean. POOH, Conduct flow check before POOH and entering the shoe.

6) Check trip with drilling BHA. Note any tight spots in DDR. Circulate hole clean and POOH.

10.5.2 Run 9-5/8″ Casing

1) Retrieve wear bushing.

2) R/U casing running tools, PJSM for running casing.

3) Run 9-5/8″ casing as per casing tally.

4) Function test floating equipment.

5) Thread lock connections of all shoe track joints.

6) Fill up casing every joint with fill-up tool;

7) Break circulation before run out of previous casing shoe.

8) Tag bottom and P/U to confirm the casing set depth.

9) Rig up cementing head, connect cementing line and pressure test same.

10) Slowly break circulation and circulate.

10.5.3 Cement 9-5/8″ casing

1) Cement 9-5/8″ casing as cement program.

2) Apply back pressure on annulus and WOC.

3) L/D 12-1/4″ BHA during WOC.

10.5.4 Install Wellhead Section C, tubing head assemble

1) Set 9-5/8″ casing hanger in 20-3/4″ 3k x 13-5/8″ 5k casing spool with required tension.

2) L/D cement head &amp;amp; landing joint.

3) N/U 13-5/8″ x 11″ 5 K casing head and P/T port seal.

4) N/U BOP stack.

5) M/U test plug and set in casing head spool. Ensure casing valve below test plug is open.

6) P/T all ram preventers, choke and kill manifolds, annular preventer, all surface BOP equipment

including kelly cocks and safety valves as per 9.5: Pressure Test.

7) Retrieve test plug and install wear bushing.

10.6 Drill 8-1/2” Horizontal Hole

10.6.1 CBL logging

1) Install wear bushing.

2) M/U 8-1/2″ directional BHA and function test same, RIH to tag TOC.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Program Page 49

3) Drill out cement plug to 7m above the 9-5/8” shoe.

4) POOH. Conduct CBL-VD L wireline logging.

5) M/U 8-1/2″ directional BHA and function test same, RIH to bottom.

6) Circulate bottoms up; P/T 9-5/8″ casing integrity as per 9.5: Pressure Test.

10.6.2 Drill 8-1/2″ Horizontal Hole

1) Drill 8-1/2" hole to TD with optimized parameters, take survey as required.

2) Wiper trip will be performed once every 300m footages drilled or depending on hole condition.

3) Circulate bottoms up. Wiper trip to 9-5/8″ casing shoe. RIH to bottom and wipe out any tight spots.

Circulate hole clean. POOH, Conduct flow check before POOH and entering the shoe.

4) Check trip with drilling BHA. Note any tight spots in DDR. Circulate hole clean and POOH.

5) RIH 9-5/8″ casing scraper to scrape liner hanger setting interval 3 times.

10.6.3 Well Completion

Refer to completion program

2. Completion Program

The completion program and production well testing includes the following parts:

 Completion fluid

 Lower and Upper Completion string

 Acidizing

2.1 Completion fluid

1） To ensure the compatibility with litho logy in order to minimize damage to the reservoir.

2） To produce minimal environmental impact

3） Allow the well to be suspended with minimum damage

The completion fluid would be designed include work fluid, perforation fluid and packer fluid, the properties of that are:

Completion fluid system: CaCl2 + KCl Clean Brine System

Density (S.G): 1.20~1.30

PH shall be kept in the range of 9.5-10

Estimated Volume (m3): 270m3

Completion fluid formulation (Density: 1.20 ~ 1.30g/cm3)

Table 2-1 Completion fluid formulation for S377 well

Name ConcentrationFunction

HEC Viscosifier 1-2 kg/m3Biocide Biocide 3-5 kg/m3Oxygen Scavenger Oxygen Scavenger 4-6 kg/m3CIM-1 Corrosion Inhibitor 5-10 kg/m3CaCl2 Weighting Material (soluble inorganic salt ) 375-475 kg/m3KCl Reservoir protect material 50-70 kg/m3H2S Scavenger H2S Scavenger 4~6 kg/m3

Note: Completion fluid formulation could be adjust as per Mud engineer request on site.

Flushing fluid: Drilling water+0.3-0.5%NaOH +0.2% Cleaning Agent

Estimated Volume: 10m3

2.2.3 Operation Procedure for Lower Completion

2.2.3.1. Well bore preparation

1) Back reaming and hole condition.

2) RIH 9-5/8” casing scraper, scrape the expected liner hanger setting depth: 2650m~2710m

and production packer setting depth: 2450m~2540m, circulate hole clean with mud.

Note: Scraper running into below the float collar is not allowed.

3) POOH scraper string.

2.2.3.2. Running 5-1/2” Screen pipe

1) Hold PJSM with all relevant personnel of rig crew and relevant personnel.

2) Rig up liner running equipment on the drilling floor and function test.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 60

3) Run 5-1/2” liner as follows:

5-1/2” guide shoe + 5-1/2” liner + 5-1/2” perforated liner + 5-1/2” liner + 5-1/2” x 9-5/8” rotating liner hanger with top packer

Note: Centralizer program recommended - For first joint of liner, one spiral rigid type centralizer at 3m below guide shoe with stop collar and one right below couple, then one centralizer for each two joints.

4) Monitor hook loads during running of the casing to detect excessive drag.

5) Make up liner hanger assembly on top of last joint of liner.

 Prior to make up the liner hanger assembly, loose the elevator to avoid the left hand

torque to release the running tool. Use the chain tong to thread before tightness.

 Don’t remove off slips; pick up around 1m to verify the connections of liner hanger

assembly.

 The first stand DP RIH, check and record the P/U weight, S/O weight. Locked rotary

table to avoid any rotation.

 All utilized 5” DP should be drifted with rabbit more than 2.56”.

 If need to rotate the string to through the tight point, limit the torque lower 5kft.lbs,

meantime stop the pump, rotate speed lower 10 rpm. Utilize the brake to control

reactive torque to avoid premature release running tool while stop rotation.

 Limit the pump pressure less than 800psi while working string up and down.

 If need to increase P/U &amp;amp; S/O weight to deal with tight pull, the maximum allowed

slack off weight is 30 Ton except liner weight, too much weight will damage the

running tool.

2.2.3.3. Setting liner hanger

1) RIH to design depth, start pump to establish circulation, if need, circulate one bottom’s up,

remove off total gas, break off the TDS and drop the ball into the string.

2) Re-make up TDS and space out. Drop ball and start pump with 2~3 bpm to chase the ball or

limit the pump pressure less than 500psi until the pressure comes up sharply.

3) Note: If the pressure comes up sharply, stop pump at once.

4) Verify the weight and recalculate the extension distance whether it is suitable.

5) Slowly pressure up to 200psi over designed set pressure hold 2 min;

6) Slack off, check the weight of the hook, if the hook load drop down continuously, it is

proved that the liner hanger has been activated, continue to slack off until set down 10~15T

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 61

running string weight on the liner hanger.

7) After the liner hanger is successfully set, commence the releasing operation procedure. If

the liner hanger cannot be set, increase 100 psi per time to repeat the setting program. Until

the pressure get same as the running tool releasing pressure.

2.2.3.4. Release the setting tool

1) After the liner hanger is successful set, set down 5T running string weight on liner hanger.

2) Pressure up to 200psi over designed, hold for 2min.

3) Bleed off, pressure up to 500psi again, pick up 1m (cannot over 2m) to verify if released.

4) If the liner weight has been lost, it indicates setting tool released successfully.

Note: If hook load continuously increase, limit over pull lower 5T than the liner weight to keep the hanger function. (If too much over pull, the liner hanger maybe un-hung)

2.2.3.5. Set top packer

1) Pick up approximately 3m, slowly slack of 15~20T to set top packer.

2) Hold 5T on packer top, close the BOP and increase annulus pressure to1000 ~ 2000psi to

check the seal ability of the packer.

Note: Control the pick up distance no more than 3m during setting top packer.

3) POOH.

2.2.3.6. Displace open-hole mud with completion fluid

1) Running 2-3/8” DP into the bottom of 5-1/2”screen to displace mud with completion fluid.

BHA: 2-3/8” DP + X/O+ 3-1/2” drill-pipe +X/O+ 5” drill-pipe

2) POOH.

3) Prepare for running upper completion.

2.2.4 Operation Procedure for Upper Completion String

2.2.4.1. Pre-Job preparation

1) Pre-connect all down-hole equipment with upper &amp;amp; lower pup joints on confirmed coupling

machine at workshop, and pressure test prior to delivery to well site.

2) Retrieve wear bushing, M/U jet sub and flush BOP stack &amp;amp; wellhead with fresh water.

3) All crossovers, pup joint for space out measured put in right place.

4) Lift control line and protectors on rig floor.

5) Lift all emergency well control subs in the right place of the rig floor.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 62

Table 2-4 Well control subs

Item Description Qty(ea) Material1 Adapter, 4-1/2" IF Box X 3-1/2" 10.2 PPF Gas tight Pin, ID: 2.922, Length:16.00" 1 L-80

6) Hold a pre-job safety meeting for running upper completion string.

Notes:

 All tubular to be run should be cleaned, inspected, drifted and tallied before run in hole.

 Review string configuration, space-out and running procedures.

 Computerized torque monitor and control equipment should be utilized for tubing

making up while running.

 Protectors for control lines should be installed as per program. Special care should be

taken while setting slips &amp;amp; picking up to prevent control lines from damage.

 Make sure that number and location of these protectors are recorded on final

completion report. Bandit clips should be kept to a minimum, all efforts should be made

to use control line protectors only. If bandit clips are used, please note number and

location.

 Ensure that covers and mats are available and utilized during RIH. Avoid any objects

dropping in hole.

 Make-up torque is recommended as follows.

Table 2-5 Make Up Torque Table

Item Description Minimum (ft-lb) Optimum (ft-lb) Maximum (ft-lb)1 3-1/2” - 10.2#,TPEX, L80 2790 3100 3410

2.2.4.2. Run upper completion string

1) P/U &amp;amp; M/U 3-1/2” re-entry guide shoe + 2.75” landing nipple as per running tally.

2) P/U &amp;amp; M/U 9-5/8” hydraulic-set retrievable production packer carefully.

3) M/U and RIH 3-1/2”SSD + 3-1/2” travel joint as per running tally (keep the SSD close)

Note: slick line test plug should be installed in SSD on the surface, and Pressure test tubing 2,000psi/15min every 1000 meter by slickline tool.

4) Run required joints 3-1/2” 10.2# TPEX tubing as per running tally.

5) Make up 3-1/2” TRSCSSV assembly be set at approximately 100m under ground level.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 63

6) Install 1/4”control line to TRSCSSV and pressure test control line 5000psi x 15mins. And

then keep 4000psi pressure while running to ensure TRSCSSV open. Install TRSCSSV

protector for the 1/4”control line.

7) Continue to run 3-1/2” 10.2# TPEX tubing as per running tally.

8) R/U slick line unit to pull out test plug. R/D slick line unit.

2.2.4.3. Space out and install tubing hanger

1) Space out as required with pup joints

2) Pick up &amp;amp; M/U tubing hanger with double pin crossover. M/U landing joint.

3) Roughly cut control line enough length to reach rig floor, and wrap 2-3 turns under tubing

hanger, then feed through tubing hanger. Connect the line back to reels, pressure test as the

same.

4) Plug off the cable port on tubing hanger.

5) Record P/U &amp;amp; S/O WT.

2.2.4.4. Land tubing hanger

1) Land tubing hanger into tubing head spool carefully.

2) Lock all tie-down bolts.

3) Close annular BOP.

4) Pressure up 3000psi /15min in annulus through kill manifold to test Tubing Hanger Seal,

observe for any pressure loss.

5) Monitor if any fluid leak from gate valve.

2.2.4.5. Set production packer

1) R/U cement unit and surface line. Pressure test.

2) Pressure up 1/4” Control Line to 5,000psi to open the TRSCSSV.

3) RIH with gauge cutter for 3-1/2" tubing. POOH.

4) RIH with 2.75" X selective testing tool and set it at Landing Nipple, leave the slick line

attached.

5) Make up the surface line and pressure test to 500psi/5mins, 1000psi/5mins, 2000psi/5mins,

3000psi/15min, to set packer, and then bleed off pressure to zero.

6) Pressure test annulus to 2000 psi/15mins to confirm the packer’s setting and sealing. Bleed

off pressure to zero slowly.

7) Pull out the 2.75" X selective testing tool.

8) R/D slick line unit.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 64

9) B/O &amp;amp; L/D landing joint.

2.2.4.6. N/D BOP &amp;amp; N/U X-mas tree

1) Close TRSCSSV.

2) Set TWCV in tubing hanger.

3) N/D bell nipple and BOP.

4) N/U tree adapter and X-mas tree. Feed through X-mas tree adapter with 1/4” control line

5) Pressure test X-mas tree 5000psi holding 10mins.

6) Retrieve TWCV and set BPV.

2.2.4.7. Secure well for rig moving out

1) Close swab valve &amp;amp; wing gate valves on both sides, open master valve and SSV.

2) Open TRSCSSV.

3) Record SIWHP (shut-in wellhead pressure), if there is any.

4) Bleed off control line pressure to close TRSCSSV.

5) Bleed off WHP (tubing pressure above TRSCSSV), if there is any, measure return

(completion fluid) volume if possible. Be aware of H2S.

6) Monitor WHP for 30 min which should be zero (report to drilling/completion supervisor if

there is any pressure build up) for leak-off test TRSCSSV.

7) Close master valve. And keep gate wing valves in close position.

8) Check the annulus pressure; bleed off to zero if there is any.

2.2.4.8. Release Rig

1) R/D all rig equipment.

2) Rig release.

3) Move out rig equipment

4) Move in well test equipment if need.

2.2.4.9. Well Suspension for Production Commission

1) Open TRSCSSV.

2) R/U slick-line unit, set 2.75”X Lock mandrel and equalizing prong at the Landing nipple,

and then pressure test the plug 5000psi*10mins.

3) R/D slick line unit.

4) R/U CTU unit.

5) Displace the diesel inside tubing above plug by CTU unit, and then R/D CTU unit.

6) Close TRSCSSSV and close the X-Tree valve as per design.

Section D: HSE Management

1. Basic HSE Requirements

1) Contractor should establish an effective HSE system and implement it strictly.

2) Contractor must adhere to the standards and regulations of Health, Safe and Environment constituted

by the local government.

3) Contractor must strictly adhere to the procedures / standards and regulations of Health, Safety and

Environment promulgated by CNPC.

4) Contractor should comply with all applicable local government regulations including guidelines and

recommended practices and any other Iranian authorities having jurisdiction.

5) All personnel should respect the local culture, traditions and social values of the indigenous

population of the Iran and South Azadegan region.

6) Contractor shall hire qualified and experienced worker and familiar his personnel with hazards

especially with H2S and Mine and UXO (unexploded ordinance) according to related procedure.

7) Contractor shall conduct proper training about UXO (unexploded ordinance) and mine awareness to

all personnel

2. Well Control

All the drilling operation shall obey the requirement of Well Control Manual of CNPC and contractor’s well control manual; Under no circumstances should the deviation from well control policy be permitted without prior approval through CNPCI (Iran) Company. If the contractor’s well control manual or anywhere has conflict with Well Control Manual of CNPC， take Well Control Manual of CNPC as the standard.

(1) Recording Reference

All BOP tests, drills, function tests, any malfunctions, repair or maintenance to the mud system and well control equipment shall be recorded in the IADC daily reports and shall be signed by both the Drilling Contractor's Tool Pusher and Drilling Supervisor on the well site. They shall also be recorded in the Daily Drilling Report.

(2) Flow Check Procedure

To avoid a kick situation, it is recommended that a flow check be conducted at specific times during the drilling operation.

1) While drilling(any warning sign)

2) Prior to tripping the drill string

3) While tripping(any warning sign)

4) While out of hole(continuously)

5) Trip out:

 After pulling 5% of the drill pipe

 At the midpoint depth of the wellbore

 Prior to pulling the first stand of drill collars

 After all the drill string is out of hole.

6) Trip in:

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 68

 Upon reaching surface casing shoe

 At midpoint of the wellbore

(3) Primary Control Reference

It is the responsibility of the Drilling Contractor to keep the hole full at all times by using all the available equipment. The Drilling Supervisor shall monitor that the correct preventive practices are being implemented and/or conducted at all times.

Extreme care shall be taken to monitor mud volume, drilling breaks and gas cut mud.

The MACP (Maximum allowable casing pressure) shall be confirmed by the Drilling Supervisor, either after setting each new casing string or, whenever the density of the drilling mud changes.

The MACP shall be clearly written on a kick control sheet that will be posted near the choke control panel.

The predetermined distance at which the Tool Joint is to be pulled above the rotary should be posted on rig floor near the BOP control panel.

The Driller is responsible for carefully measuring and recording the slow pump pressure. Awareness of these values is an important element in killing operations, in order to avoid formation breakdown.

Take a measure and record the slow pump pressure at three different flow rates with all pumps at the following times:

 Once per tour.

 Whenever changes occur in the mud density/rheology.

 Whenever changes occur in the dimension and characteristics of the string, BHA, jet size, jet

plugged or jet lost, etc.

(4) Secondary Control Reference

Any time a drilling break is noticed, drilling shall stop and a static monitoring of the well shall be carried out. Space-out for all Pipe Rams and Annular Preventer must be known to the Driller at all times to keep tool joints clear of annular preventer and rams.

(5) Kick Detection Procedure While Drilling

If there is an indication of a possible kick occurring, the Driller shall follow the kick detection procedures outlined below:

Stop drilling with the pump onPick up top drive to a predetermined space-out positionShut off the pumps and check for well flow by visual examination on the bell nipple or flow line.

1) If the well is not flowing:

 Immediately notify the rig manager, Drilling Supervisor and Tool pusher.

 The Drilling Supervisor will decide whether to circulate bottoms up or resume drilling. The

penetration rate will be limited as long as the cause of the abnormal situation is undetermined.

Always be aware that some hydrocarbons (gas or oil cut mud) may rise to the surface. Ensure that

the proper monitoring and safety systems all in place.

2) If the well is flowing:

 Close the well as per the Shut-in Procedure.

 Immediately notify the Wellsite Drilling Supervisor, rig manager and Tool pusher.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 69

3. Health

1) This well is committed to develop for oil in a responsible manner that promotes employees' and local

inhabitants' health.

2) A trained Paramedic and proper MEDIVAC equipment and ambulance shall be stationed on the

drilling rig during the drilling campaign.

3) An emergency medical evacuation plan shall be coordinated, and shall keep in touch with emergency

response centres manned by qualified doctor 24-hours per day in Tehran or Ahwaz city.

4) Medical emergency report and investigation shall be performed as soon as possible in accordance

with Medical Emergency Procedures.

4. Safety

1) Contractor shall make detail operation procedures which have covered all related activities.

2) Due to high concentration of H2S in Sarvak, Kazhdumi and Gadvan reservoirs, Make detail H2S

operation procedures, in particular, and carry it out strictly.

3) Contractor must provide lifesaving equipment like SCBA, Escape Mask, H2S detectors (Multi

portable and personal detector) and implement the H2S Services system during drilling activity.

4) All lifting equipment and cranes must be certified by approved 3rd party (approved by Institute of

Standards &amp;amp; Industrial Research of Iran)

5) All electrical equipment should be designed for such locations, and listed by nationally recognized

testing laboratory. All wiring components and electrical equipment should be maintained in

accordance with the manufacturers’ recommendation.

6) Contractor shall implement the risk assessment and Job Safety Analysis before starting the drilling

activity.

7) PSSR (Pre-Startup Safety Review) before spud-in and penetrating into oil and gas formations are

mandatory by contractor under the supervision of Company and approved by Company.

8) Contractor shall pay much attention to fire, explosion prevention and well control.

9) Contractor shall make detail Emergency Response Plan and Evacuation Plan.

10) Contractor shall do efforts to keep employees safety as possible.

5. Environment

Requirement on location

1) The access road and well location construction shall be accorded with construction program of well

location. It minimizes any impacts on the local residents, prevent all soil contamination, prevent all

ground water contamination, and prevent all surface water contamination. Cutting and waste water

pit shall be lining and drainage system shall be good and to avoid farm and season canal

contamination.

2) Management regulation for transportation truck, drilling operation machine and tools shall be

carried out strictly to avoid damage farm and trees, etc.

3) Well site shall be flat and clean. All the equipment, materials, drilling tools shall be put in order.

4) Control the rig noise to decrease the influence to surroundings while drilling at the inhabitation area.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 70

5) Contractor shall perform on‐site segregation of wastes prior to transfer to the transfer area and/or

delivery to the transportation Contractor in accordance with the following waste hazard

classification.

Non-Hazardous Wastes (Re-usable, Recyclable and Recoverable Wastes)

1) Ferrous and non-ferrous metals, plastics, and wood wastes, PETs and potentially paper and glass

2) Materials shall be separately segregated and provision may be made for size reduction and

containerization equipment.

3) The Contractor/Generators are encouraged to seek for recycle/re-use alternatives based on the Local

available facilities, for final disposal of these materials.

4) It should be noted that landfill disposal of metal waste materials and wood wastes shall be prohibited.

For wood wastes re-use of and/or salvage of commercial timber, or deliver/release to

5) local community are recommended.

Hazardous Wastes

Hazardous Waste, such as clinic waste, oil waste, waste produced by painting, insulation works shall be duly segregated.

Facilities of environment protection supplied to well site

1) Mud purifying circulation system

2) Cooling water spraying and circulating system for drilling pump

3) Special recovery tank for deposed oil

4) Reserve house for valuable mud chemicals

5) Automatic mud weighting supply system

6) BOP stack is equipped according to drilling design

7) Define a separation area for gathering all waste according to waste management procedure

Requirement of environment protection on location

1) Sewage pit Area of the sewage pit: 1500 m2

2) Prior to spud， mud pit and sewage pit must comply with the standard. Install cooling water spraying

and circulating system for drilling pump and sewage circulating system for cleaning rig floor and

equipment.

3) Install BOP stack and pressure test shall be operated according to the drilling design to avoid

blowout.

4) Install oil supply lines and check whether there is any leakage.

Management of environment protection on location

1) In drilling operation， keep mud and sewage from overflowing. If it happens, clean up timely.

2) Mud chemicals shall be stored according to the standard and shall not be scattered around well

site. If mud chemicals scatter while loading or unloading, clean it up at once. The sack of mud

chemicals shall be recovered. Do not dispose it on site.

3) Enhance water management at well location and do not wash equipment with fresh water. Stop

water flowing for long time. Overhead tanks shall be utilized.

4) Oil management shall be carefully at the well site. No oil dirt on site, no dripping and leaking at

oil tank and manifold system area.

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 71

5) After the well is completed, reinforce the sewage pit， solidify the disposed mud and have

environment procedure fulfilled.

6) Install fuel supply lines and keep them in good condition.

7) All the drilling fluid materials shall be recovered and shall not be left at the well site after

completion.

8) The environmental protection personnel from contractor and operator side shall check and accept

the well site environment according to the regulation after completion. Only well site

environment is qualified, the well can be delivered.

9) Transition Area for Hazardous Waste shall also be provided by each Contractor in his allocated

area,

Company shall approve each area before use as well. The transition area of hazardous waste shall

be:

 Paved, fenced, marked and illuminated

 Equipped with appropriate Fire Extinguisher and other emergency response equipment

 (e.g. Eye-Wash and Emergency Shower, appropriate First Aid kits, neutralizer substances

etc.).

 In compliance with the requirements of handling, transportation and storage of hazardous

waste (based on usage chemical MSDS).

 Configured to accommodate a safe physical separation of incompatible waste materials (e.g.

acids, and alkalis, organic materials and strong oxidizing agents).

 Capable enough to provide storage for the entire range of hazardous wastes generated during

the work.

 The area shall be provided with Spill Containment and means of preventing run‐ off to and

from the area.

 All waste containers to be stored shall be appropriately labeled.

 Hazardous Waste shall be transferred in a timely manner, within a maximum period of 48

hours, for any period in excess of this permission shall be granted from Company and/or

PEDEC.

Standard of utilization and discharging of wastes on location

1) Conduct drilling operation according to the design, drilling mud is needed before spud.

2) Sewage that cannot be recovered shall be put into the pit， do not discharge. If the volume of sewage

is too big and some should be discharged， the quality of drainage must comply with the following

regulations.

Maximum permitted concentration of wastes in water

Number Items ItemsConcentration (mg/L)1 pH 6～9 4 Volatilization powder 0.52 Crude oil 10 5 Sulfide 13Floating objects100 6Oxygen consumed by Chemicals100

Concentration Number

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 72

3) Make full use of waste water. Water in mud pump and water brake must be utilized by circulating.

Water that is used to wash drill floor, shall be recovered after deposition. The recovery rate of

waste water shall reach 85％.

4) Disposed oil shall be recovered entirely. Recovery rate shall be 100％.Dumping of waste oil shall

be prohibited.

5) In drilling operation, non-chrome thinner high temperature mud system shall be used often if

engineering is qualified.

6) While drilling in special area， adopt mud creation inhabitation techniques to decrease the waste

mud creation.

7) After completion, all the mud chemicals, bentonite, clays shall be recovered and shall not be left at

the well site. Mud in purifying tank shall also be recovered after completion.

8) In case of any flammable or noxious gas encountered， steps shall be taken at once to prevent kicks

and blowout. Gas shall be guided into ignition equipment to burn it away.

9) Burning material that may result in serious smog or horrible smell at the well site is not allowed.

For cement or barite that may result in granular dust pollution， operation shall be performed in a

closed system.

10) For abandoned well， filled with cement plugs to seal the oil, gas or water formation.

11) Contractor/Generators are required to transport all generated wastes to the location/facilities

designated, and licensed by authorities and/or approved/verified by the Company/PEDEC (as

appropriate).

12) All wastes shall be transferred by approved Sub‐ Contractor (which could be a 3rd party

Sub-Contractor of the Contractor/Generator itself). The Waste Transporter and any other waste

management service Contractor shall be licensed in accordance with local regulations and are in

compliance with such regulations. Company shall verify and approve the Waste Transportation

Contractor by checking licenses and qualifications, audit and inspections, verification of human

resources and received trainings etc.

13) The transportation Contractor shall ensure that all wastes are transported in a safe and responsible

manner.

6. H2S monitoring and prevention measure

6.1 Monitoring H2S

1) Somewhere on the well site is apt to accumulate H2S, such as mud pit, shale shaker, and drilling

platform, should be installed one set of. Make sure H2S monitor and alarm system can be used.

2) Alarm threshold setting for H2S warning indicator: the first warning level is 10mg/m3, unable

warning sound, just show present value of H2S content to operators. The second warning level is

20mg/m3, when the H2S content in air exceed 20mg/m3, H2S warning indicator can give an alarm

automatically, and warning sound should be heard by everyone work in well site. The audible alarm

should be installed on Kelly board.

3) Equip enough gas masks on drilling platform, shaker screen (total amount should large than 24), and

equip 12 sets of 30min positive pressure Air Aspirators， 2 sets of portable H2S monitor and

SOUTH AZADEGAN PROJECT

S377H on Pad 50 Drilling Program

Version: 3rd

Drilling Department Page 73

appropriative H2S warning indicator. Arrange a person specially assigned for taking charge all the

protective apparatus. This man must do periodical check to keep these equipment in a good

condition.

4) H2S monitor should be done weekly check and compulsory examination.

5) After drilling into the pay zone, strengthen the detection of H2S content in mud.

6) Contractor shall implement H2S service system by himself or 3rd party for controlling the risk of H2S

leakage.

6.2 Measures for H2S prevention

1) Windsocks shall be set on the drill floor and in the monsoon direction or/and prevailing wind

direction of Azadegan oil field, off well site. In emergency, employees must follow the evacuation

procedure.

2) Explosion-proof blowers shall be installed around the rig, shale shaker, and drilling fluid tanks to

blow away toxic and flammable gas at the well site.

3) The rig crew in the H2S area shall be furnished with H2S monitors and adequate protective apparatus

in accordance with SY/T 5087. Everybody shall be able to use, maintain, and check the apparatus.

4) The technicians of rig crew are responsible for H2S proof training and the tool pusher is in charge of

the supervision and examination.

5) The pH value of drilling fluid in the H2S area shall be kept over 9.5. Pay attention to measurement of

H2S concentration in drilling fluid. The H2S concentration shall be kept at less than 50mg/cm3 by

using sulphur removal agents and degassers.

6) Personnel safety and protection shall be carried out according to SY/T5087 during operations in

polluted areas where H2S content exceeds the critical concentration.

7) When the rig crew makes decision to bleed and fire because well control cannot be performed due to

well site conditions, operations shall be performed according to procedures in SY/T5087.

8) Once blowout occurs, the incident shall be reported to the higher level department as soon as possible

and fire fighting trucks, ambulances, medical care personnel and technicians shall be on duty at the

well site.

9) After the blowout is controlled, H2S concentration should be measured in the places where H2S is

possible to gather.

10) The personnel are not allowed to access until the H2S concentration is below the critical

concentration.

1 Introduction

1.1 Program Introduction

The South Azadegan Oil field has been discovered by Well AZN-001, drilled in 1999. This well drilled to a total depth of 4,643m evaluating all the possible hydrocarbon bearing of the Cretaceous sequences. So far up to, 39 wells have been drilled in the South Azadegan Oil Field.

Carbonate reservoir of Sarvak formation of the Middle Cretaceous age, was penetrated and evaluated in all the initial 6 exploration wells (AZN-001, AZN-002, AZN-003, AZN- 004, AZN-005 and AZN-006) and has been broadly developed in Azadegan Oil Field. Moreover hydrocarbon potentials of the clastic oil bearing zones of Kazhdumi and Gadvan Formations were also evaluated in the early exploration phase of the field. Clastic oil bearing zones of Gadvan formation has only been developed in the crestal area and southern parts of the Azadegan Oil Filed.

1.2 South Azadegan Structure

As the Previous exploration/delineation drilling in Azadegan oil field has proved presence of a very prolific hydrocarbon bearing sedimentary column, particularly within the Cretaceous sequences. The Fahliyan Formation was divided into 6 zones, named as Fah-0, Fah-1, Fah-2, Fah-3, Fah-4 and Fah-5, of which Fah-1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 bear light oil. The average thickness of the Fahliyan, based on well data, is 299m. The oil bearing zones of Fahliyan only developed in south part of the field around Well AZNS-004 and AZNS-006. 4 wells, AZNS-004, 006, 010 and 011, penetrated Fahliyan oil bearing zones.

Figure 1: Azadegan Oil Field

Pad-56 has been nominated as a location in which AZNS-022 (Sarvak horizontal producer) well has been drilled, from this pad. F3 (Fahliyan vertical well) and S388 (Sarvak horizontal well), shall be drilled in this pad. This pad is located on middle of Azadegan oil field in Block II Error! Reference source not found. shows position of the Pad-56. The resent scope of work for Pad-56 location is to drill a vertical well (F3) in Fahliyan reservoir and subsequently to be completed as a Fahliyan carbonate reservoir oil producer.

6 Key Reservoir Description

Fahliyan Formation

The Fahliyan Formation in south Azadegan Oil Field is generally composed of a sequence of limestones, off-white to cream to light brown wackestone-packstone, occasionally detrital, fossiliferous and in places with intergranular and micro-vuggy. The Upper parts of the section, with over 105 meters in thickness, as has been designated as Upper Fahliyan as has low reservoir quality.

The Lower Fahliyan lower section is composed of clean, light cream to light brown limestone with interbeds of dolomitic limestones. Towards the base the lithology changes into more argillaceous limestone.

9 Rig Move

 Hold pre-move meeting on location. Discuss contingency plans for:

o Safe arrangements for rig up and rig down.

o Safe standby areas for rig in case move have to be suspended.

o Loss of trucks in case of repair.

o Moving at night.

 Hold pre-move safety meeting. Ensure everyone understands individual

Responsibilities.

 Commence rigging down. The ordering of trucks will be done by rig manager.

 Commence rig move to AZNS 045, F 03, PAD No. 56

 Position rig for optimal operations

 Rig up on cellar

 Finish rig up and commence operational procedure for AZNS 045

 Before leaving previous location, all equipment should be checked.

 Test all manifold and surface safety valves

o RKB – GLE and Elevation

o Spud date and time.

 Mud tanks should be empty during move. Fill water tanks and keep as full as

possible.

10 Pre Spud

1) Certificate

 All rig equipment are on location, properly rigged up and function-tested.

Check availability and content of hoisting equipment inspection &amp;amp; maintenance records

according to API.

2) Safety Device

 Test draw-works crown block saver and record results on daily drilling report;

test derrick man escape device (Geronimo line) and record time required for test weight on

daily drilling report; test rig intercom system, especially for Driller-Derrick Man and DrillerMud Logger communication link.

 Check that all rig safety equipment, e.g. fire extinguishers, eye-wash stations

etc., is installed at the proper locations as per rig layout diagrams.

 Verify that substructure is level and foundation adequate to support

drilling unit during whole well period and that mast is centred above rotary table.

 Ensure rig alignment with Top Drive System. Make sure rig tongs and rig

floor hoisting equipment are adequate for handling 8-1/2″ and 9-1/2″ drill collars. Make sure

that mechanical tong torque gauges for drill pipe/drill collar are properly functioning and have

been calibrated; re-calibrated as required.

3) Rig Equipment

 Verify that the 26″ tri-cone bit is ready to be picked up, all nozzles installed,

and 26″ bit breakers are on the rig floor. Make sure single-shot or multi-shot survey tools

available for vertical section survey.

 Make sure all solids control equipment are correctly installed and have been

function-tested with charging pressure gauges in place where applicable. Make sure adequate

screen inventory for the shale shakers is available in the sizes required for the well. Make sure

water tanks are completely filled and transfer lines from water tanks to mud system are

hooked up. Make sure all mud materials and chemicals required to drill 26″ section are on

location, in sound condition and suitably stored. Make sure that bentonite is pre-hydrated and

sufficient volume has been built for top section and for well-kill.

 Make sure that all mud pumps have been fitted with new consumable parts:

liners, pistons, valves, seats etc. Make sure that pulsation dampeners are charged with

Nitrogen to the proper pre-charge pressure, 30% of expected standpipe pressure during

drilling of the 26″ hole section; check if sufficient Nitrogen bottles are available on location to

adjust pulsation dampener pre-charge pressure as required. Make sure that mud pump pop-off

valves are set at the proper pressure for the liner sizes installed and that the mud discharge

lines from the pop-off valves to the mud tanks are properly secured. Check that all highpressure hoses are properly secured with safety chains. P/T should be done to high-pressure

surface line of circulating system.

NIDC

NIDC Drilling Projects Management

Drilling Engineering &amp;amp; Programming Division

South Azadegan Oil field

AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3 PEDEC

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03) Page 21

4) Wellhead &amp;amp; BOP

 Have 2″ line pipe nipple with 2″ ball valve welded to 30″ conductor pipe 0.2m

above cellar bottom to drain cement from conductor/riser pipe after cementing 20″ surface

casing. Verify that pre-installed 30″ conductor pipe is vertical and that no angle/bend exists

between conductor and 30″ riser pipe.

 Make sure that adequate supply of 20″ casing is on location, inspected and

ready to be run, float shoe should already be made up, centralizers should be pre-installed on

the pipe-rack prior to spud in the well.

 Ensure mud logging unit is functioning and equipment are calibrated and tested

properly.

5) Others

 Verify that the rig’s fuel tanks are full to meet minimum of three days rig

consumption available on site.

 Make sure all waste treatment equipment available and commissioning test is

ok.

 Hold pre-spud meeting with relevant service Company personnel on location.

Discuss procedures involved throughout 26″ hole section drilling, reporting system and

critical safety operation measures.

NIDC prepared a pre-spud checklist and distributed it in rigs. The checklist will be performed based on the aforementioned checklist. In addition to the checklist, following matters should be checked:

 Check &amp;amp; observe the alignment of the rig. This check will be performed in

four stages; rig levelling, derrick verticality, derrick alignment with rotary

table, rotary table alignment with conductor pipe. To do this pick up 3

joints of 9 1/2” DC and check the verticality of the derrick.

 Mechanical facilities for mud treatment such as :

Desander, Desilter, mud cleaner, proper size of shaker–screens, hoppers, mud guns &amp;amp; mixers, should be all available on rig and functioning properly.

 All mud tanks, mud transfer lines &amp;amp; connection should be checked by

filling them with water &amp;amp; cross wellhead circulation.

 Mud engineer laboratory should be equipped with a complete set of testing

equipment. The calibration should be done under supervision of well site

drilling supervisor before starting the operation.

 Weight indicator, rotary speed gauge, any smart drilling instrumentation,

Top Drive, Stroke meter, pressure gauges on manifold and in the drilling

console, loss / gain gauge and any other gauges should be checked and

calibrated.

 Stand pipe manifold valves &amp;amp; connection must be checked &amp;amp; pressure

tested.

 All motors should be checked under load prior to start drilling.

 Check &amp;amp; control mud stock &amp;amp; cement stock every day &amp;amp; if there is any

necessity to request mud material, cement or any drilling tool, it should be

prepared &amp;amp; requested at proper time.

1 STRUCTURAL GEOLOGY:

The Azadegan oil field is located at 80km west of Ahwaz and the Azadegan Field is an elongated anticline, trending in N-S direction, belongs to Arabian Trend which is different from “Zagros Trend”. There is no outcrop for Azadegan structure and it was detected by 3D seismic interpretation.

2 LITHO-STRATIGRAPHY DESCRIPTION:

The stratigraphy sequence of the field includes Aghajari, Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan Formations, from top to bottom. This sequence in well no. AZN-10, the deepest up to now drilled well of the field, continues with Garau, Gotnia, Najmah, Sargalu, Alan, Mus and Neyriz formations.

Fars Group

Fars group originally divided into two formations, Aghajari and Gachsaran formations.

Aghajari Formation

Depth: Surface to 1105m Drilled Thickness: 1105m Age: MIOCENE-PLIOCENE Lithology: This Formation begins with light cream, light gray, yellow, reddish brown, quartz, subrounded, good sorted Sandstone. This formation is mostly consisting of red marl interbedded with sandstone and red claystone layers. The Red claystone increase toward the base of the formation. The Sandstones are variably consolidated and generally light brown, light gray in colour, fine to medium grain size, generally fine grained, rarely coarse grained, generally well sorted and dominated by sub-angular to sub-rounded quartz grains, loose, friable to moderately hard, slightly calcareous. The Marls are red brown to brown and pale red brown, generally sticky and slightly silty, soft to firm and locally very low calcareous grading to claystone. The Red claystones are brown, moderate to dark brown, generally soft, sticky and amorphous, locally some calcareous grading to marl, generally non silty and occasionally mottled. A fauna of brackish to freshwater was observed, together with more marine fauna.

Gachsaran Formation

Depth: 1105-1454m Drilled Thickness: 349m Age: Middle Miocene - Early Miocene Lithology: This Formation begins with white, glassy, soft, medium, hard Anhydrite and off white, light gray to gray, soft to medium hard, Dolomitic Limestone. Comprises of anhydrite interbedded with gray marl and red marl, salt beds of variable thickness and some thin limestone beds. This formation in type section not divided to other member. The Anhydrite is white off white, milky, soft, partly crystalline to crystalline, medium hard to hard. The Gray marl is gray to light gray, soft. The Red marl is red brown

NIDC

NIDC Drilling Projects Management

Drilling Engineering &amp;amp; Programming Division

South Azadegan Oil field

AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3 PEDEC

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03) Page 25

to brown, soft, sticky. The Limestone is gray to light gray, buff to buff gray, hard, slightly dolomitic.

Asmari Formation

Depth: 1454-1754m Drilled Thickness: 300m Age: Oligocene - Miocene Lithology: This Formation begins with cream, light gray, soft to medium hard, poor Fossil Dolomitic Limestone. Consisting of white, off white, light gray to gray, soft to medium hard, I/II,I, II, Dolomitic Limestone particularly in upper part. The Sandstone increase toward the base with interbedded gray marl, dolomite and claystone of the beds formation. The Sandstone is gray, light to gray, medium to coarse with partly pyritic, medium sorted. The Gray marl is gray, light gray, soft. The Dolomite is light cream to cream, porous. The Claystone is red light brown, soft, sticky.

Pabdeh Formation

Depth: 1754-2184m Thickness: 430m Age: Paleocene-Eocene

The Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field.

Lithology: This formation starts with Marl, gradually replaces by Argillaceous Limestone. The upper part is made of grey to dark-brown Argillaceous Limestone and Marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in Oligocene. The lower part is composed of dark-brown, slightly Argillaceous Limestone. Pelagic fauna, mostly Globigerina and Globorotalia, indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to late Eocene in age. It seems that no early Paleocene deposits exist in the Azadegan area.

Gurpi Formation

This is formation consists of three main units:

A) Upper Gurpi Member

B) Tarbour Tongue

C) Lower Gurpi Member

Upper Gurpi Member

Depth: 2184-2325m Drilled Thickness: 141m Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian

Lithology: This Member begins with light gray, gray, off white, soft to medium, sticky Argillaceous limestone. The Upper Gurpi Member is mostly consisting of gray buff to gray with of trace partly buff gray, dark gray, medium hard to hard argillaceous, high argillaceous graded to marl Argillaceous limestone interbedded with anhydrite marl and chalky limestone beds. The Anhydritic marl is light gray to gray, soft, sticky, graded

to high argillaceous calcareous. The Chalky Limestone is light gray to buff gray, medium hard to soft,chalky II,A slightly argillaceous.

Tarbour Tongue

Depth: 2325-2403m Drilled Thickness: 78m Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian Lithology: This Member begins with cream, light brown, porosity, oil stained Limestone. Tarbour Tongue consisting of cream to light gray, light brown, buff, medium hard, II,A Limestone interbedded with chalky limestone and marl beds. The Chalky limestone is light cream, soft. The Dolomite medium bedded dark gray. The Marl is light gray to gray, soft, sticky.

Lower Gurpi Member

Depth: 2403-2528 m Drilled Thickness: 125m Age: Campanian- Maastrichtian Lithology: This Member begins with off white, cream, soft to medium hard Argillaceous limestone and light gray to gray, soft, sticky Marl. The Member consisting of dark gray to gray, light gray, soft to medium hard Argillaceous limestone and light cream and soft. II, A. Chalky Limestone beds.

Bangestan Group

Bangestan group is divided into four formations: Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak and Kazhdumi.

Ilam Formation

Depth: 2528-2622 Drilled Thickness: 94m Age: Coniacian -Santonian Limestone. This formation is mostly consisting of limestone interbedded with shale and clay stone beds. The Limestone is cream, buff to buff gray, light brown, soft to medium hard, chalky, II, A, Oil stained Partly slightly argillaceous. The Shale is green gray to light green gray, mostly brown gray, medium hard, pyritic, slightly calcareous. The Clay stone is green gray to light green gray and soft, slightly calcareous.

Laffan Formation

Depth: 2622-2628 m Drilled Thickness: 6m Age: Coniacian

Lithology: This Formation begins with greenish gray, green, Soft, sticky, pyrite, slightly and calcareous Claystone and green, light greenish gray, light gray, medium to hard, fissile Shale. This formation is mostly consisting of shale interbedded with clay stone beds. The Shale green gray to light green gray, mostly brown gray medium hard, pyritic, medium hard, slightly calcareous. The Clay stone green gray to light green gray, soft and slightly calcareous.

Sarvak Formation

Depth: 2628-3277m Drilled Thickness: 649m Age: Cenomanian

Lithology: This Formation begins with light brown, cream, light cream, I/II, I and Oil stained Limestone. This formation is mostly consisting of off white, cream, buff to cream and white to cream, soft, soft to medium hard, chalky Limestone interbedded with claystone, argillaceous limestone, shale, argillaceous dolomite and dolomite limestone beds. The Claystone is gray, soft, sticky, high calcareous. The Argillaceous Limestone is white gray to light gray, light buff, medium hard and slightly bituminous. The Shale is dark brown, dark gray, black, medium hard and calcareous. The Argillaceous Dolomite is buff gray, hard. The Dolomitic Limestone is buff gray to light brown gray, hard, densely. The Dolomitic Limestone is gray, partly light gray, medium hard.

Kazhdumi Formation

Depth: 3277 - 3484m Drilled Thickness: 207m Age: Albian

Lithology: This Formation begins with dark brown, soft to medium hard, some fissile and calcareous Shale. This formation is start with dark brown to dark green gray, medium hard to hard, soft to medium, high bituminous, calcareous Shale interbedded with argillaceous limestone, sandy claystone, claystone, marl, sandy limestone, limestone beds. The Argillaceous Limestone is dark brown to brown gray, medium hard to hard, micro bituminous. The Sandy claystone is light green gray, soft, high calcareous. The Claystone is gray to light gray, soft. The Marl is green gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Limestone is dark green gray, hard, faint sandy. The Limestone is light gray to green gray, medium hard to soft, partly argillaceous. The Kazhdumi Formation at base is consisting of Azadeghan Member.

Khami Group

Khami group is divided into three formations: Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan.

Dariyan Formation

Depth: 3484 - 3645m Drilled Thickness: 161m Age: Aptian

Lithology: This Formation begins with off white, light cream, I/II, mottled Limestone. This formation is mostly consisting of white to light cream, off white, white to light gray, medium hard Limestone interbedded with light gray to gray, light gray, green gray medium hard to hard, partly crystalline carbonaceous, pyritic, partly sandy Argillaceous Limestone beds.

Gadvan Formation

Depth: 3645 - 3716m Drilled Thickness: 71m Age: BARREMIAN -APTIAN

Lithology: This formation is mostly consisting dark green gray to green gray, soft, sticky, partly calcareous Shale and light gray, green gray, soft, sticky Claystone interbedded with argillaceous limestone, marl, sandstone, sandy claystone beds. The Argillaceous Limestone is green gray to light gray to white, pyritic hard. The Marl is green gray, soft and sticky. The

Sandstone is brown, gray to brown, sorted, rounded, hard, calcareous, oil stained. The Sandyclaystone is light gray, soft and with quartz grains.

Khalij Member

Depth: 3716 - 3727m Drilled Thickness: 11m Age: BARREMIAN -APTIAN

Lithology: The member comprises of light gray to gray, medium hard, partly pyritic Argillaceous Limestone and white, white to light gray, hard Limestone beds.

lower Gadvan Formation

Depth: 3727- 3933m Drilled Thickness: 206m Age: BARREMIAN -APTIAN

Lithology: This formation is consisting of alternating of marl, argillaceous limestone, limestone, claystone, sandy claystone and sandstone beds. The Marl is green gray, soft, sticky, slightly calcareous. The Argillaceous Limestone is gray white gray medium hard to soft, partly pyritic. The Limestone is white, white to light gray, hard. The Claystone is light gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Claystone is light gray, soft, sticky, fine sand grained. The Sandstone is brown to gray, partly white buff, gray, friable, sorted, subrounded,medium quartz grained, partly calcareous.

Fahliyan Formation

Depth: 3933 - 4125m Drilled Thickness: 192m Age: NEOCOMIAN

Lithology: This formation is start with light gray, light green gray, soft Limestone interbedded with green gray, soft, sticky Marl, green gray, light gray, soft, Argillaceous Limestone and dark green gray, soft, sticky Claystone beds. This formation at continue is consisting of buff white light gray, light brown, brown, medium hard, hard, densely, slightly argillaceous Limestone and off white, white gray, medium hard Chalky Limestone beds.

request.

1. Nearby wells

1.1 AZN 1

36" HOLE SECTION (0-98M) SUMMERY:

Drilled with 26” Bit and Mud Weight: 65 PCF to 98m (30” CSG Shoe), Enlarge Hole by 36” Hole Opener to 98m, Run 30” Casing String to 98 m and Cementing till CMT Slurry Return to the surface.

26" HOLE SECTION (98-1349M) SUMMERY:

Drilled with 26” Bit and Bentonite Mud Weight 68PCF F/ 98 to 1139m While Circulate Bentonite Pill around Well Periodically for Hole Cleaning, Due to Tight Hole at 1139 Increased MW F/ 68 to 70PCF. Continued Drilling to 1349m (20 “CSG Shoe) while increased to 74 PCF Due to Tight Hole and Circulate LCM Pill Periodically for lost controlling. While Hole Condition, observed Caving and Tight Hole so, wash and ream Several Time. Therefor Increased MW to 76PCF. Ran Logging Tools to 753m, took weight at This Point. Repeated condition the Hole w/ 5BPH Loss, Due to Tight Hole Increased MW to 79 PCF. Ran GR Log, then, ran 26” Reamer in Hole, Run 20” Casing String to 1349 m and Cemented till slurry return to surface.

17½ " HOLE SECTION (1349M- 1776M) SUMMERY:

After Drilled out Shoe Track, Displaced Mud System F/ 79 to 120PCF, Commenced Drilling F/ 1349 t0 1381m, Increased Mw F/ 120 to 130PCF, continued Drilling to 1776m (13 3/8” CSG Shoe) w/ 1-4 BPH lost, Increased Mw F/ 130 to 131PCF. While Trip out observed 30 – 50 K LB over pull and while trip in wash and ream F/ 1397 to bottom.

12" HOLE SECTION (1776M- 3330M) SUMMERY:

Displace Mud System F/ 131 to 74 PCF, Drilled out Shoe Track, began drilling F/ 1776 to 1926m While short trip observed tight hole. So, increased Mw F/ 76 to 77 PCF, continued drilling to 2123m w/ 2- 3BPH Loss. Suddenly observed oil on cutting sample therefore increased MW to 80 pcf, continued drilling to 2600 m W/ 2-5BPH lost with circulated LCM Pill periodically. Ran FDC/ CNL/ NGT/ GR and DLL/ MSFL/ GR Log. continued drilling to 2791m while increase ROP, detected 30% Gas, return in pipe connection increased Mw to 85PCF continued drilling to 2976m w/ 4BPH Loss while Circulated LCM

Pill Periodically. Ran LDT / CNT / NGT, DLL / MSFL / GR / SONIC and RFT Logs (RFT Record Formation Pressure of 30 Point of ASMARI and SARVAK Formations and sampled from formation fluid sampling of 2 Point F/ SARVAK Formation). Continued Drilling to 3330m w/ 3-4BPH Loss (9 5/8” CSG Shoe). Run LDT / CNT / NGT / AMS, GR / SONIC and FMS Logs, ran reamer trip while reaming stuck pipes at 1993m, free pipes w/ 75000 KLB O. Pull. Run 9 5/8” casing string to 3330 m and cemented.

8 ⅜ " HOLE SECTION (3330M- 4036M) SUMMERY:

Drilled out Shoe Track, Ran CBL / VDL Log, Displace 85PCF mud by 135 PCF, Commenced Drilling F/ 3330 to 3412m, due to mud loss circulation decrease Mw to 128PCF while drilling, Stuck Pipe at 3363m while Trip Get Free w/ 100KLB O. Pull, Increased Mw to 130 PCF, continued drilling to 3439.8m w/ 1BPH loss. Due to fish in well and could not recovered, side track well. Continued drilling to 3445m, due to tight hole weight Mw to 133PCF. Continued Drilling to 3485.5m While Cut Mw to 115PCF, Due to Tight Hole Increased Mw to 125PCF. Displace Mud System F/ WBM to OBM Mw 115PCF at 3485.5m (Note: While Pipe Connection Stuck Pipes and Free Just by ASID). Continued Drilling to 3490 m, DST Well, Cut Mw to 85 PCF, Continued Drilling to 3720m While Increased Mw to 87PCF, Continued Drilling to 4036m, OBS Well Flow w/ 4BPH and Detect 99.5% Gas, Increased Mw to 100PCF, Hole Condition &amp;amp; Ran 7” Liner to 4035.5m (L.L. at 3178.5m) and Cemented.

5⅞" HOLE SECTION (4036M- 4643M) SUMMERY:

Commence Drilling w/ 100PCF OBM to 4165m w/ NL-NG. Due to kick 50BBLs, increased Mw to 133PCF. Continued drilling to 4350m w/NL- NG. Observed cut mw 131.5PCF at 4350m in Return, Increased Mw to 134 PCF. Continued Drilling to 4443m w/ NL- NG. OBS 16 BPH Loss at 4443m, so pumped 5PPB LCM to circulation system and continued drilling to 4643 m (TD).

1.2 AZNS 05

26" HOLE SECTION (0-119M) SUMMERY:

This well drilled as delineation well to Gadvan formation and began drilling this section by 26” bit and 65 pcf mud weight in Aghajari formation. Then, Ran the 20” casing at the depth of 119 m, and cemented to the surface.

NIDC Drilling Projects Management

Drilling Engineering &amp;amp; Programming Division

South Azadegan Oil field

AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3 PEDEC

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03) Page 37

17 ½ " HOLE SECTION (119M- 1538M) SUMMERY:

Drilled by 17 ½” bit and 65-80 pcf mud weight in Gachsaran formation to the depth of 1226 m with NL/NG. Increase MW to 80 pcf due to high torque at 1517 m. As condition trip observed tight hole problem at 1300 m and passed by W/R and pumped 100 bbl Hi.Vis Pill with 6 bph lost. Then, set the 13 3/8” casing at 1538 m and cemented with (98 bbl, 115 pcf) "A" class.

12 ¼ ″ HOLE SECTION (1538M-1930M) SUMMERY:

Drilled by 12 ¼” bit and 80-112 pcf mud weight in Gachsaran formation to 1602 with NL/NG. Continued drilling to the depth of 1570 m and suddenly bit took weigh so increase mud weight from 80 to 88 pcf. Increased MW to 95 pcf at 1610 m due to oil contaminated mud and salt water on shaker and increase Ca++ and PH in return mud. Because of tight hole problem in 1634, 1690 and 1750 m increased mud weight finally to 110 pcf. Then, took LCS/GR/BGN/CDR logs from 1928 to 1500. Ran 9 5/8” casing at the depth of 1930 and cemented by ‘D’ class (550 bbl, 120 pcf) cement.

8 ⅜" HOLE SECTION (1930M- 3916M) SUMMERY:

Drilled by 8 3/8” bit and 74 pcf mud weight in Asemari formation. Continue drilling to 2250 m in Pabdeh, Gurpi, and Ilam and Laffan formations with NL/NG. Took 3.5 m core from Sarvak formation at 2826 m. then, displaced water base mud with 76 pcf oil base mud and continued drilling to 2854 m which drill string was stuck and got free by 50 klb over pull. Increased MW to 80 pcf and took 17 and 25 m core from 2854-2871 and 2890-2917.5 m intervals. Continued drilling to 3075 with 2-10 bph lost so pumped Hi.Vis Pill periodically. Took DLL/MSFL/GR/LDT/CNL/NGS/BHC from 3087 to 1900 m. continued drilling Kazhdumi and Darian formations to 3618 m and observed oil contaminated and gas saturated in return mud so, circulated to balance Mw to 76 pcf. Continued drilling to in Gadvan formation and observed Gas cut mud in 3980m and 4107 m so increased MW to 84 and 86 pcf. Drilled to 4051m and due to gas cut mud and decreased MW had to increase and circulated mud with weight 110 pcf but Not Succeed so, set bridge plug and squeezed 32 bbl ‘D’ cement. Then took DLL/MSFL/BHC/GR/LDL/CNL/NGT Logs from 3916m to 2965 m and RIH RFT tools and recorded 72 points pressure. Finally ran 7” Liner and hanged from 1835.5 m to 3916 m and cemented by 977 sks ‘D’ cement.

5⅞" HOLE SECTION (3916M- 4210M) SUMMERY:

Drilled by 5 7/8” bit and 115 pcf mud weight to 4210 m in Gadvan formation with pumped Hi.Vis periodically. Took CBL/VDL logs from 3890m to 1837m. Ran 9 /58” DST tool. Ran DLL/MSFLBHC/GR/LDL/CNL/NGT logs from 4210m to 3916m. Ran 5” liner at shoe depth of 4105m and liner lap at 3846m. Then, cemented by ‘E’ class (20 bbl, 120 pcf) and after dry test and set the packer at 3816 m took CBL/VDL logs from 3844m to 1837 m. finally ran completion 3 ½” tubing and set its packer at 3839.95 m and cemented by 7.2 bbl, 120 pcf ‘E’ class cement. Further perforated 3975 to 3979 m interval and implementation acidizing program and installed ¾” production chock. Rig released at 19.09.1381.

1.3 AZNS 18

26" HOLE SECTION (0-100M) SUMMERY:

Drilled by 26" Bit from surface to 100 m in Aghajari formation with Bentonite mud (MW=67 PCF). Then, ran the 18 5/8"casing and cemented while returned slurry to the surface. The casing shoe was at a depth of 100 m.

17 ½ " HOLE SECTION (100M- 1370M) SUMMERY:

Drilled by 17 ½" Bit with salt water mud (MW=70-85 PCF) in formation to 1370 m with 2-3 bph lost. Then ran 13 3/8" casing and cemented while 44 bbl cement slurry return to surface. Casing shoe was placed at the depth of 1370m and cemented meanwhile 90 bbl slurry returned to the surface.

12 ¼ ″ HOLE SECTION (1370M-1821M) SUMMERY:

Drilled with 12 ¼ " bit and 115-125 pcf, salt water mud in Gachsaran formation to 1821 m. Because of observed increase of Ca++ (mg/lit) in return mud, so increase MW to 117 pcf by Barite. Then, ran 9 5/8” casing, placed at 3904 m. Then, cemented it by 650 SXS (S.V:209 bbl, S.W:118 pcf).

8 ½ " HOLE SECTION (1821M- 3176M) SUMMERY:

Drilled by 8 ½" bit and 73-82 pcf mud weight to 2106 m with 5-6 bph mud lost. So, pumped LCM and increased mud weight by displacing with oil based mud to 82 pcf. Continued drilling and at a depth of 2743m, suddenly pipe string cute then, catch and pull out of hole by overshot. Also drilling string stuck at

Run 12 1/4" bit &amp;amp; tag TOC @1405m, D/O top plug &amp;amp; hard cmt to 1420m ( float collar ), take pressure test, OK, D/O BTM plug &amp;amp; float collar &amp;amp;

12 ¼ ″ HOLE SECTION (1445M-3880M) SUMMERY:

Drilling of hole section was started from 1444.5MT and continued to 1702mt with KCl/Polymer type mud (density 75 PCF)with2-12 bph loss in formation. POOH to surf. obs wash out on 8" D.Jar body, RIH Then drilling was continued from 1702m to 2170mt with 1-4 bbl. /hr. loss in formation by increased mud Weight from 75 to 77PCF considering to hole conditions to obviate tight holes is several spots. Re-Entry Operation: This Operation was performed for D/O CMT plugs, Perforation &amp;amp; Completion. After

N/U BOP 10K, 13 5/8, D/O CMT plugs number 3, 2 and one (mentioned in the last hole) was performed with 8

1/2" bit(105 pcf). RIH to 7" liner lap. POOH to surface and change the bit. RIH with 5 7/8" bit to 4111m, took

weight. D/O Hard CMT F/4111m to 4133m (LDC depth). Continue D/O to 4138.5m (7m above Shoe). POOH &amp;amp;

RIH w/CBL/VDL/CCL/GR to 4138m, log up F/4138 to 3784.9m. perform Scraper trip. Scrap 3790 to

4138m. MW was increased to 107 pcf. Pump 50 bbl Hi-Vis Pill &amp;amp; spot 57 bbl perforation fluid (86 pcf). RIH

w/ DST-TCP tools to 4086m. Test pump truck w/6000 psi. RIH CCL/GR logging tools to 3748m, Correlate

depth. POOH to surface. Set RTTS PKR @3874.7m, test PKR W/300 psi on annulus, ok. Open LPR inc.

press to 2000 psi to Ann, gun firing by applying 5500 psi, no succ. try to gun firing w/5500 psi, Obs build up

pressure to 2000 psi gradually. Open RD-Circ valve by applying pressure f/ 2000 to 3300 psi, then close LPR

by decreasing pressure to 600 psi. pressure was released to zero. Commence Rev Cir 3 cap of spring w/107

pcf mud. balance MW on 107 pcf. while dump 80 bbl contaminated mud w/ oil &amp;amp; gas (85 pcf, 8% oil). unset

PKR POOH to surface, Obs all gun operated successfuly. RIH w/ 5 7/8" R.Bit to 3720m, Circ BTMs up, Obs.

returned cut MW to 98 pcf (8% oil), balanced MW to 106 pcf. Due to some events that happened, fish (wire

line instruments &amp;amp; PKR tail) was remained in hole and after long time fish was recovered. Rec. Completion

Fluid (107 pcf). RIH w/ J.Mill 4 1/8" to 4105m, pump 50 bbl Hi Vis Pill, 50 bbl wash pill and displace [[H.MW](http://H.MW)]([http://H.MW](http://H.MW))

(105 pcf) w/ completion fluid (107 pcf) w/ 300 psi back pressure. POOH &amp;amp; L/D J.Mill. Completion string

was run &amp;amp; pressure test w/8000 psi was performed several times. Ret check valve from SSD (After Reverse

Circ as two Cap, Ret. check valve). Close SSD. Install check valve in NO-GO. Set PKR. test PKR &amp;amp; TBG

hanger to 2000 psi , OK. Final Pressure test completion string was performed. P/O Landing Nipple check

valve from NO-GO. install TWCV in TBG Hanger. Close 3SV. Pressure Test TWCV (8000 psi), ok. N/D

BOP Stack 13 5/8", 10K. install Bottom master valve &amp;amp; X-Mas &amp;amp; test it, OK. Remove TWCV. Open all

valves except BTM master valve. Released Rig @ 06:00 on 02-Feb-2017.

17 1/2" Hole Section:This section was started from 150 to 1410m. with Bent. Mud (67-73 pcf) &amp;amp; 2-24 BPH

loss in some times.Based on the mud program, this section must be drilled with KCL Polymer mud, but the

shortage of the materials causes to using the high vis bentonite mud. because of the inappropriate mud, tight

hole was happen several times , increase MW to 80 pcf &amp;amp; NACL to 280000 (mg/l) No Succ. Logging

operation (CDR/GR) was failed due to tight hole (just run to 420 m). Finally, KCL Polymer mud (82-85 pcf)

was used and makes normally hole condition.13-3/8"casing string was run from surface to 1409.5m (120

Jts, N-80, 68 ppf, BTC). Pumping cement &amp;amp; displace with 682 bbl mud (MW: 84 pcf) was performed. 42 bbl

cement slurry was returned to surface. Bumping was observed W/ 1500 Psi. check back flow, OK. 20 3/4"

3K, 13 5/8" 5K C.H.S was installed. Run 12 1/4" bit and tag TOC @ 1385m. Drill out cement plug, float

collar &amp;amp; cement below the float collar to 1402.5 m. Pressure test 13 3/8" casing string with1600psi, OK.

Drilling out cement was perfomed and float shoe to 1409.5m.
