 Paved, fenced, marked and illuminated

 Equipped with appropriate Fire Extinguisher and other emergency response equipment

 (e.g. Eye-Wash and Emergency Shower, appropriate First Aid kits, neutralizer substances

etc.).

 In compliance with the requirements of handling, transportation and storage of hazardous

waste (based on usage chemical MSDS).

 Configured to accommodate a safe physical separation of incompatible waste materials

(e.g. acids, and alkalis, organic materials and strong oxidizing agents).

 Capable enough to provide storage for the entire range of hazardous wastes generated

during the work.

 The area shall be provided with Spill Containment and means of preventing run‐off to and

from the area.

 All waste containers to be stored shall be appropriately labeled.

 Hazardous Waste shall be transferred in a timely manner, within a maximum period of 48

hours, for any period in excess of this permission shall be granted from Company and/or

PEDEC.

Standard of utilization and discharging of wastes on location

1) Conduct drilling operation according to the design, drilling mud is needed before spud.

2) Sewage that cannot be recovered shall be put into the pit， do not discharge. If the volume of

sewage is too big and some should be discharged， the quality of drainage must comply with the

following regulations.

Maximum permitted concentration of wastes in water

3) Make full use of waste water. Water in mud pump and water brake must be utilized by

circulating. Water that is used to wash drill floor, shall be recovered after deposition. The

recovery rate of waste water shall reach 85％.

4) Disposed oil shall be recovered entirely. Recovery rate shall be 100％.Dumping of waste oil

shall be prohibited.

5) In drilling operation, non-chrome thinner high temperature mud system shall be used often if

engineering is qualified.

6) While drilling in special area， adopt mud creation inhabitation techniques to decrease the waste

mud creation.

7) After completion, all the mud chemicals, bentonite, clays shall be recovered and shall not be left

at the well site. Mud in purifying tank shall also be recovered after completion.

8) In case of any flammable or noxious gas encountered， steps shall be taken at once to prevent

kicks and blowout. Gas shall be guided into ignition equipment to burn it away.

9) Burning material that may result in serious smog or horrible smell at the well site is not allowed.

For cement or barite that may result in granular dust pollution， operation shall be performed in

a closed system.

10) For abandoned well， filled with cement plugs to seal the oil, gas or water formation.

11) Contractor/Generators are required to transport all generated wastes to the location/facilities

designated, and licensed by authorities and/or approved/verified by the Company/PEDEC (as

appropriate).

12) All wastes shall be transferred by approved Sub‐Contractor (which could be a 3rd party SubContractor of the Contractor/Generator itself). The Waste Transporter and any other waste

management service Contractor shall be licensed in accordance with local regulations and are in

compliance with such regulations. Company shall verify and approve the Waste Transportation

Contractor by checking licenses and qualifications, audit and inspections, verification of human

resources and received trainings etc.

13) The transportation Contractor shall ensure that all wastes are transported in a safe and

responsible manner.

6 H2S monitoring and prevention measure

6.1 Monitoring H2S

1) Somewhere on the well site is apt to accumulate H2S, such as mud pit, shale shaker,

and drilling platform, should be installed one set of. Make sure H2S monitor and

alarm system can be used.

2) Alarm threshold setting for H2S warning indicator: the first warning level is

10mg/m3, unable warning sound, just show present value of H2S content to

operators. The second warning level is 20mg/m3, when the H2S content in air exceed

20mg/m3, H2S warning indicator can give an alarm automatically, and warning

sound should be heard by everyone work in well site. The audible alarm should be

installed on Kelly board.

3) Equip enough gas masks on drilling platform, shaker screen (total amount should

large than 24), and equip 12 sets of 30min positive pressure Air Aspirators， 2 sets of

portable H2S monitor and appropriative H2S warning indicator. Arrange a person

specially assigned for taking charge all the protective apparatus. This man must do

periodical check to keep these equipment in a good condition.

4) H2S monitor should be done weekly check and compulsory examination.

5) After drilling into the pay zone, strengthen the detection of H2S content in mud.

NIDC

NIDC Drilling Projects Management

Drilling Engineering &amp;amp; Programming Division

South Azadegan Oil field

AZNS#045 (F 03 – Area II ) Rev 0.3 PEDEC

Drilling Program WELL NO. AZNS# 045 (F 03) Page 155

6.2 Measures for H2S prevention

1) Windsocks shall be set on the drill floor and in the monsoon direction or/and

prevailing wind direction of Azadegan oil field, off well site. In emergency,

employees must follow the evacuation procedure.

2) Explosion-proof blowers shall be installed around the rig, shale shaker, and drilling

fluid tanks to blow away toxic and flammable gas at the well site.

3) The rig crew in the H2S area shall be furnished with H2S monitors and adequate

protective apparatus. Everybody shall be able to use, maintain, and check the

apparatus.

4) The technicians of rig crew are responsible for H2S proof training and the tool

pusher is in charge of the supervision and examination.

5) The pH value of drilling fluid in the H2S area shall be kept over 9.5. Pay attention to

measurement of H2S concentration in drilling fluid. The H2S concentration shall be

kept at less than 50mg/cm3 by using sulphur removal agents and degassers.

6) Personnel safety and protection shall be carried out according to SY/T5087 during

operations in polluted areas where H2S content exceeds the critical concentration.

7) When the rig crew makes decision to bleed and fire because well control cannot be

performed due to well site conditions, operations shall be performed according to

procedures in SY/T5087.

8) Once blowout occurs, the incident shall be reported to the higher level department as

soon as possible and firefighting trucks, ambulances, medical care personnel and

technicians shall be on duty at the well site.

9) After the blowout is controlled, H2S concentration should be measured in the places

where H2S is possible to gather.

10) The personnel are not allowed to access until the H2S concentration is below the

critical concentration.

بخش سیالات

نظر کارفرما: به نوع مواد مورد استفاده برای هرزروی در حفره های 17-1/2و 12-1/4اشاره شود.

جوابیه: در صفحه 73و 77برنامه چاه به نوع مواد مورد استفاده برای هرزروی در حفره های 17-1/2و 12-1/4اشاره

شده بود.

نظر کارفرما: در مخزن از فروبار نباید استفاده گردد و مخزن باید با گل لایمستونی حفاری شود لذا فروبار از جداول 77و 77حذف

گردد.

جوابیه: برنامه سیال این چاه فهلیانی، مطابق برنامه سیال چاههای فهلیانی قبلی و تایید شده توسط کارفرمای محترم

تنظیم شده است. لازم به توضیح است که افزایش وزن سیال توسط لایمستون تا بالاتر از ،100 PCFمنجر به افزایش

شدید رئولوژی سیال شده و همچنین مصرف لایمستون و درصد مواد جامد سیال به طور غیر متعارفی افزایش مییابد.

شایان ذکر است که در چاه 047میدان آزادگان جنوبی وزن سیال حفره 5.7بدلیل مشاهده جریان تا 111 PCFافزایش

یافته است، لذا در صورتیکه فقط از لایمستون در این محدوده وزنی استفاده گردد، یقینا رئولوژی سیال با مشکل مواجه

خواهد شد. با توجه به موارد ذکر شده برنامه سیال چاههای فهلیانی جهت افزایش وزن تا نزدیکی 100 PCFبا لایمستون

بوده و در ادامه با فروبار خواهد بود.

بخش سیال تکمیلی:

نظر کارفرما: صفحه : 177

مطلبی در مورد سیال تکمیلی نوشته نشده است.

جوابیه: برنامه سیال تکمیلی به طور جداگانه ارسال شده است.

بخش پسماند:

برنامه تصحیح گردید و در صفحه 143آورده شده است که از 70درصد آب دیواتر برای گلسازی استفاده شود.

Petrophysic Comments:

This is a drilling program. In actual condition some operations such as logging may be changed and some logs aren’t performed.

Geological Comments:

Wellhead Coordinate has been changed according to client comment.

GENERAL INFORMATION Page 8 of 117

Development Well

Introduction

The AZADEGAN Oilfield is Located in Khuzestan Province and is Approximately

Located 80 Kilometers West of Ahwaz City, Near to Iran-Iraq Border. It is in The Northwest

of The YADAVARAN Field and in The West of JUFEYR Field. The Average Monthly

Temperature is 45ºc in summer, and Highest Temperature is 52ºc. The Lowest Temperature is

-4.4ºc in winter. Maximum Rainfall is 78 mm in February, Winter Season.

The Field is in Flooding Plain Area with Convenient Road Network which Extends to

Ahwaz. Most of The Oil Fields in The Region are Located in The Foothill Zone Zagros Folded

Zone of the NW-SE Trending Zagros Mountain Range, and Have Similar Trends to The Zagros

Mountain Range Elongated Anticline Structure.

This Document Covers the Drilling of AZNS-??? (S358) Sarvak Development Highly

Deviated Well With pilot hole in Sarvak in South Azadegan Oilfield, Iran. Well S-358 Shall Be

Considered to Produce from the Sarvak Reservoir. Plan is to Drill the Pilot Hole to Depth of

±3058 Mt in 8-1/2” Open Hole, and Perform Logging as program. Then Plug back to ±30 Mt

above Kick off Point.

GENERAL INFORMATION Page 10 of 117

Development Well

S358 Highly Deviated Well with Pilot Hole in Sarvak

Drilling on Pad-40 Objectives

As The Previous Exploration/Delineation Drilling in AZADEGAN Oil Field has Proved

Presence of a Very Prolific Hydrocarbon Bearing Sedimentary Column, Particularly within the

Cretaceous Sequences. The Sarvak Formation is the Main Reservoir which is divided to 13

Zones (Sarvak-1 to Sarvak-12 and Sarvak Intra). The Zones of Sarvak-1 to Sarvak-10 are Oil

Bearing with Tilted WOC.

A Location on Pad-40 has also been selected for Drilling Sarvak Producer Wells with

pilot hole. As offset well, AZNS-8 was Drilled and Completed Previously. This Pad is Located

in the Northern Part of Field in Block-I (Figure1) Shows the Location of Pad-40 on the

Underground Contour Map at the Top of Sarvak 3&amp;amp;4-6 Reservoir Zone.

This Well will be Drilled As a Highly-Deviated Well in Sarvak and Pilot Hole in Sarvak

Reservoir. This Document Presents the Scope of Work of S358 as Highly Deviated Producer

Well with Pilot Hole.

The Objective For this Well will Be Evaluating Sarvak formation in pilot hole and

Interest Sarvak 3&amp;amp;4-6 in deviated hole and Providing Maximum Drain Hole for Production, So

Following the Critical Coordination in Trajectory Is Essential.

Figure 1

Pore and Fracture Press Prediction

Since the Formation Pressure of Gachsaran Formation is Different between North Area

and South Near and Decreases Greatly and Complete Loss Took Place at all Four Existing

Wells in South Area, the Oilfield is divided into three Areas.

Area I (North of AZN-10): Area I Located in North of Line1(Y-Coordinate: 3452051),

in this Area, Gachsaran Formation is Characterized with Abnormal High Pressure. For No

Wells have ever been Drilled in the Area between Line1 (Y-Coordinate: 3452051) and Line 3

(Y-Coordinate: 3457993), This Area Exists Greater Uncertainties and is Delimited to the Area

I Temporarily.

Area II (Between AZN-10 and AZN-11): Area II Located between Line1 (Ycoordinate:

3452051) and Line 2 (Y-Coordinate: 3443549), in this Area, the Formation of Gachsaran

Formation Decreases Greatly.

Area III (South of AZN-11): Area III Located the South of Line 2 (Y-Coordinate:

3443549), in this Area, the Formation of Gachsaran Formation Decreases Greatly and all

Existing Wells Occurred Complete Loss at Pabdeh Formation.

The Area Division shall be Adjusted According to Further Actual Drilling Operation,

and Quantity of Wells in Different Areas could be also Adjusted Accordingly.

GENERAL INFORMATION Page 12 of 117

Development Well

Program Outline

It is planned to drill and complete a Sarvak highly-deviated development well with pilot

hole.

26" Hole section:

The 26” Hole Section will be Drilled 154 Mt through Aghajari Formation. Aghajari

Formation Consists of A Marl, Sand-Shale. For Drilling This Section 67 pcf Hi-Vis Bentonite

Mud will be used for Hole Cleaning. Also PHB Pill Will Be Used to Clean the Hole and Since

Bentonite has the Best Cake around the Well Bore, It will make a Good Cake for Hole Stability.

According to Previous Wells in this Field, this Section is almost Problem Free. The 20" Casing

will be set at ±154 Mt and it will be cemented in One Stage with Stinger (Exact CMT Program

will be issued in Due Time). N/U 20-3/4" 3k C.H.H &amp;amp; 21 1/4" 2K Annular BOP and Single

Rams.

17-1/2" Hole section:

This Section will be drilled from ±154 Mt to ±1275 Mt through Aghajari Formation.

According to offset Wells tight spots, Hard Back Ream, W&amp;amp;R while RIH and Bit Balling Up

in surface was recorded. For Drilling This Section 70-80 Pcf Kcl/Nacl Polymer Mud will be

Used. Also PHB Pill will be used to clean the Hole. CDR/GR log will be run in open hole. 13

3/8" Casing Shoe will Be Set at ±1275 Mt at the Top of Gachsaran to Case off Full Soft

Unconsolidated Formation of Lower Aghajari and Drill the next Hole Section with different

Mud Density. It will be cemented in two Stage (Lead &amp;amp; Tail - Exact CMT Program will be

issued in Due Time). N/Up C.H.S 20-3/4" 3k x 13-5/8” 5K. N/U 13-5/8" 5K BOP’s Stack (13

5/8" 5K Annular Preventer + 13 5/8" 5K Double Rams + 13 5/8" 5K Single).

12 ¼" Hole Section:

This Section will be drilled from ±1275 to ±1577 Mt through Gachsaran. For Drilling

this Section the 95-107 Pcf KCl Polymer Mud can be used. The Risks of Drilling this Section

is Heavy MW &amp;amp; High pressure formation, Low ROP, Tight Hole and Stuck Pipe Based on

Offset Wells. CDR/GR Log Will Be Run in Open Hole. 9 5/8" Casing Shoe will be set at ±1577

Mt at the Top of Asmari to Isolate High Pressure Formation for Drilling to Next Section to TD

with low Mud Weight. It will be cemented in two Stage (Lead &amp;amp; Tail -Exact CMT Program

will be issued in Due Time). CDR-GR-DT &amp;amp; CCL/GR/CBL-VDL Will Be Run. N/U T.H.S 13

5/8" 5k x 11" 5k. N/U 13-5/8” 5K BOP’s Stack. (13 5/8" 5k Annular BOP + 13 5/8" 5k

Double Rams + 13 5/8" 5k Single Ram).

8 ½” Hole Section (Vertical):

This Section will be Drilled from ±1577 to ±3058 through Asmari, Pabdeh, Up. Gurpi,

Tarbur, Lo. Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak as vertically pilot hole. 74-80 Pcf Kcl Polymer Mud

Will Be Used For Drilling This Section. The logging will be performed as program in pilot

Well : AZNS??? (S358)

GEOLOGICAL INFORMATION Page 23 of 117

Development Well

Formation Description

A brief description of the stratigraphic units expected to be drilled in South Azadegan

typical well has been presented in the following breakdown.

 Recent/Aghajari Formation

The Aghajari Formation consists of a repetition of brown to grey calcareous Sandstones,

chert debris and gypsum-veined red marl. A fauna of brackish to freshwater was observed,

together with more marine fauna.

 Gachsaran Formation

The Gachsaran Formation with a thickness ranges from 400 m to 600 m, thicken towards

the north. Deposition of the Gachsaran Formation is cyclic. The cycle began with a deposition

of shallow-water marine carbonates, and then it was overlain by a thick evaporitic phase. The

evaporites were capped by red to variegated marl, which represented the end of cycle.

 Asmari Formation

The Asmari Formation in Azadegan Field developed a massive sandstone interval, with

a few thin argillaceous layers in the upper sections. Sands are clear, fine to medium-sorted, and

poorly consolidated. On the other hand, the lower sections consist of intercalation of sandy,

chalky, slightly dolomitic high-energy limestone with sands layers.

 Pabdeh Formation

Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field. The upper part is

made of grey to dark-brown argillaceous limestones and marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this

part. was deposited in Oligocene. The lower part is composed of dark-brown, slightly

argillaceous limestone. Pelagic fauna, mostly Globigerina and Globorotalia, indicates that this

part was formed during late Paleocene to late Eocene in age. It seems that no early Paleocene

Formation exists in the Azadegan area.

 Gurpi Formation

The Gurpi Formation in the Azadegan Field consists of grey to dark-brownish, slightly

argillaceous micritic limestone with Maestrichtian to Campanian aged fauna. At Well AZN-1,

about 50 meters of chalky limestones were encountered in the middle of the Gurpi formation,

which contains abundant fragments of rudists, bryozoa, corals, and Orbitoides.

 Ilam Formation

In the Azadegan Field, the Ilam Formation consists of grey micritic limestone, often

argillaceous, rarely pyritic, limestones. Microfauna, mostly pelagic, including rotalids and

oligostegina assemblage, a few algae, bryozoa, and rudist fragments exist.

Well : AZNS??? (S358)

Type : ISHPS

GEOLOGICAL INFORMATION Page 24 of 117

Development Well

 Laffan Formation

The Laffan Formation developed a thin greenish-grey pyritic shale layer, capping the

Sarvak Formation. It was encountered in the Azadegan wells and only ostracods were found.

It is a cap rock for the upper part of the Sarvak reservoir.

 Sarvak Formation

The Sarvak Formation developed a carbonate unit which was gradually overlying the

Kazhdumi Formation, and it was abruptly terminated by an erosion phase and covered by

various late Cretaceous sediments. A Typical geological section of the Sarvak Formation in

Azadegan reveals that dark grey fine grained nodulary argillaceous limestone interbedded with

thin marls in the bottom, and graded upward into a white-to-buff massive chalky limestone with

siliceous nodules; it was developed a very massive tan-to-brown limestone containing abundant

rudist debris in the middle part; the upper part is made up of rubbly limestone with red staining

and leached zones of breccia and the top of Sarvak Formation is very ferruginous

NEARBY WELL Page 27 of 117

Development Well

Drilling Risks &amp;amp; Pit falls

 While drilling in massive salt formation of Gachsaran Formation, be careful of well

trajectory and the salt’s cave-in/dissolving.

 Formation gas from Asmari, Pabdeh and Sarvak formations containing harmful

hydrogen sulfide (H2S) has the risks to harm site personnel during the operation. The

H2S content in Sarvak Formation is 1750 ppm according to the sample analysis. All site

personnel should pay more attention on H2S hazards, and all H2S equipment should be

in good condition.

 Be aware of the bit collision, borehole wall sloughing and bit stuck.

 Lost circulation is the main downhole problem in this part of south Azadegan field, such

as AZN-5 and AZN-1. The losses occurred in Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam and Sarvak

formations.

HSE Concerns

 In the well AZN-06, there was chert in the cutting in Pabdeh Formation from 1900-

1950m and from 2070 to 2090m. Also there was chert in well AZN-12 in Pabdeh

Formation from 1950-1975m and from 1570-1585m; and in Sarvak Formation from

2825-2865m; and in Dariyan Formation from 3450-3475m. Also these cherts are not so

thick to damage the bit; but, wellsite geologist should consider the case during drilling.

Based on these intervals, the chert occurences in well F13 could be from 1550-1580m,

1900-1980m, 2000-2100m, 2800-2880m and 3420-3470m.

 According to South Azadegan# 27 well test results, H2S Content is about 160-2436 ppm

and CO2 content is about 2.6 - 5.44 mole% in Sarvak Reservoir which have to be

considered as potential hazards.
